# Decision log — running paper trail

> **Purpose.** An append-only, chronological record of every **decision / issue / bug / fix / pivot** so that a future session (after auto-compact or a premature end) can reconstruct *what was decided and why* without re-deriving it from faded context. This file exists because on 2026-05-30 the assistant recommended re-opening an **already-settled** decision (build v0.2 vs. a v0.1.3 patch-sweep) — reasoning from in-context memory instead of the durable record. The user: *"we will not have this problem again that you decide something against an already decision line we are already into."*
>
> **Binding usage (see CLAUDE.md "Decision-log discipline"):**
> 1. **Before re-opening ANY decision** — search this file + [`v0.1.1-self-test-findings.md`](v0.1.1-self-test-findings.md) + tasks.md for it. If it's here marked **SETTLED**, do not re-litigate it; execute it. Surface a change only if you have *new* evidence, and say explicitly "this revisits SETTLED decision D-N because <new evidence>".
> 2. **After making/learning anything durable** — append an entry in the SAME batch as the work. Decisions, issues found, bugs, fixes, design choices, pivots. Newest block at the TOP.
> 3. Entry types: **DECISION** (a choice + why), **PIVOT** (a previously-documented choice changed — preserve the old per decision-trail rule), **ISSUE** (a problem noted, maybe not yet fixed), **BUG**, **FIX**, **NOTE**.
> 4. This is the running paper trail. Stable rules still graduate to CLAUDE.md; task state still lives in tasks.md; per-PR narrative still goes to the build log. This file is the chronological spine that ties them together.

---

> **Older entries live in the archive.** **D-1 … D-306** — every decision made BEFORE the v0.5.0 cut — were relocated to [`DECISION-LOG-archive-pre-v0.5.md`](DECISION-LOG-archive-pre-v0.5.md) on 2026-07-28 (Task 249), byte-identical and in the same newest-at-top order. **This file holds D-307 onward** (D-307 IS the "tag v0.5.0 now" decision). Both files are `D-nnn` anchor sources for `validate-docs`'s `references` family — it globs `docs/journey/DECISION-LOG*.md` — so a citation to any entry, archived or live, still resolves.

## 2026-08-08 — D-440 · FIX — D-432 shipped "dark by default" as `prefers-color-scheme`, which handed the default back to the OS — the toggle completes it

**The defect, in the user's words:** *"looks the same to me"* — on a light-preferring machine, which is what they run. D-432 was implemented as `:root` dark under a `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` override, so a light-OS reader got a page byte-identical to the one before the redesign and never saw the dark design the decision commissioned. The `:root[data-theme="light"|"dark"]` blocks existed and were tested — but **nothing in the page ever stamped the attribute**, so the "explicit choice wins in both directions" contract was a hook with no hand on it. The ask: *"we need a button to switch between dark and light."*

**The fix.** An inline script in `<head>` — above the body, because position IS the feature here: run it at the bottom and a reader who chose light gets a dark flash on every load — stamps `data-theme` on `<html>` from `localStorage['cmk-viewer-theme']`, falling back to **`dark`**, and **never consulting the system preference**. A **button in the header chrome** beside the read-only pill flips and persists it, labelled with the WORD for the theme the click moves to (a moon glyph in the corner of the chrome is the control nobody finds) and carrying an `aria-label` that states the action. Persistence is best-effort and the flip is not: a blocked-storage profile still gets the theme it clicked. The wiring is guarded (`if (window.cmkTheme)`) because every view on that page renders from the one script — an unguarded reference would cost the reader their whole memory to save a button.

**The CSS was CUT, not layered.** The media block is deleted rather than left as a no-op layer: since the stamp runs on every load it could never apply again, and CSS asserting "the OS decides" is CSS stating the policy this reverses. `[data-theme="dark"]` is deleted too — `:root` already is dark, so it overrode nothing. Four root blocks became **two, each palette written exactly once**.

**The drift guard got STRONGER, which is the part worth recording.** D-432 needed a byte-identical-duplicates test because each palette shipped four times; the honest move on deleting the duplicates was to retarget that test, not to delete it with them. It now (a) pins the root-block structure to exactly `[':root', ':root[data-theme="light"]']`, (b) checks the light override's token SET against **the dark palette itself** rather than against another copy of light — so a token added to `:root` and forgotten in light now fails, which two agreeing copies structurally could not catch — and (c) requires every theme-varying token to appear **exactly twice** in the sheet, which is what stops a third copy coming back. The AA test kept both themes and gained the toggle's own pair (`--ink-2` on an `--ink-3` tint, the one element whose text sits on a wash of a different ink than itself). The theme module is small enough to be COMPILED AND RUN in the test against a fake `document`/`localStorage`, so the default, the stored-choice round trip, the junk-value fallback and the blocked-storage path are behavioural assertions rather than greps — a first for this page, which has no DOM environment by the §24.1.7 zero-dep contract. _Relates Task 268, D-432, design §24.1.2._
## 2026-08-08 — D-439 · FIX — Task 264's two hygiene items, and the "low-harm tiebreak" turned out to be a dead stage (still only half-alive after the fix)

**(a) The raw NUL, second instance → a check instead of a third reminder.** `graph-index.mjs` carried 5 literal NUL bytes (first at offset 15988, shipped in PR #335) as the join separator in two composite keys — `rebuildEdges`' edge map and `traverseLinks`' dedup set. Correct behaviour, invisible in every editor, and enough to make `grep` classify the whole file as binary, so every content search silently skipped it. Replaced with the `\u0000` escape (byte-identical strings) plus the reason the separator has to be NUL at all, matching the fix Task 261's self-review applied to `semantic-backend.mjs`. **Two instances is a class with a deterministic shape, so it graduated to a check** — `tests/source-hygiene.test.js` scans every tracked text source via `git ls-files` and names the offending file + byte offset. It went red on `graph-index.mjs` before the fix. A validator script was considered and rejected as heavier than the rule deserves: one assertion over `git ls-files` runs in the suite that already gates every push.

**(b) `rerankResults`' temporal term was not a wrong tiebreak — it was dead, and the benchmark that judged reranking was measured through it.** `Math.abs(r.created_at * 1000 - target)` on a value that is ALREADY epoch ms (index-rebuild's `isoToEpochMs = Date.parse`) put every difference ~3 orders of magnitude past the 45-day window, so `Math.max(0, …)` clamped the boost to exactly 0 on **every** row: the stage documented as "keyword-overlap 0.30 + temporal 0.40" had only ever been the keyword half. The same seconds-vs-ms error the sibling `semanticRowPassesFilters` fixed one block up (Task 227), traced to PR #150.

**Measured, one session, same corpus + embedder, only the one-line change stashed between runs** (`npm run bench:recall`): `hybrid-rerank` NDCG@10 **0.816 → 0.846** overall, and the **temporal** question-type **0.750 → 1.000**. Plain `hybrid` on the same day is 0.860/0.750. **The consequence for the record:** the 0.816 in the [2026-07-19 fusion benchmark](../research/2026-07-19-fusion-benchmark.md), cited twice since as evidence against reranking, was measured with the temporal term dead. That note is a frozen record and is NOT edited; the correction lives here. It does not overturn D-360's KEEP-CURRENT verdict — reranked still loses to plain hybrid overall — but anyone re-opening reranking should start from 0.846, not 0.816.

**AMENDED at review, and the amendment is the load-bearing part — the term is only PARTLY alive, and this entry's first version over-claimed.** The fix is necessary but not sufficient, because of where `created_at` survives fusion: `search.mjs:533`'s keyword projection **does not emit `created_at`** at all, while the semantic backend's row shape does (`semantic-backend.mjs:736`); RRF (`search.mjs:740`) is **first-seen-wins with the KEYWORD list iterated first** (`bench-recall.mjs:354` passes `keywordResults` first). So any document returned by **both** backends is represented in the fused list by its keyword row and carries no `created_at` — **the temporal term is still structurally dead on every dual-backend row**, and fires only on semantic-EXCLUSIVE ones. That is why the boost changed anything at all, and it bounds how much it could ever change.

**The 0.846-vs-0.860 attribution is therefore OPEN, not settled.** The first version of this entry asserted "the remaining loss belongs to the keyword-overlap term"; that was reasoning past the evidence. Two candidate causes remain live and this measurement does not separate them: **(i)** the keyword-overlap term genuinely costs ranking, or **(ii)** the temporal term is still dead on the overlap rows — the ones most likely to be top-ranked — so the stage is still running at partial strength. Separating them needs a run where `created_at` survives fusion on every row (carry it in the keyword projection, or make RRF prefer the field-richer row), which is a pipeline change, not a measurement. **Small-n caveat, stated because the numbers look more precise than they are:** the corpus is 40 entries / 17 queries and the temporal question-type is **n=2**. The runs are deterministic and the deltas exact — but a 0.750 → 1.000 move on two questions is two questions, not a trend.

**No user-visible change, stated plainly rather than assumed:** `rerankResults` has exactly one consumer, `scripts/bench-recall.mjs`'s `hybrid-rerank` pipeline. No `cmk` verb and no MCP tool calls it — `search()` never imports it — so nothing a user runs behaves differently today, and no CHANGELOG entry was written for either item. **The methodological lesson, kept because it fired twice in one task:** the first claim ("unreachable even after the fix") came from probing the keyword path alone and generalising; the correction ("reachable on semantic-origin rows") was right about reachability but silently assumed the *rows the benchmark actually ranks* carry the field — and a reviewer reading the fusion ORDER, not just the row shapes, found the third answer. Reading one path gives you a hypothesis; reading how the paths COMPOSE gives you the behaviour. _Relates Task 264, Task 261 (surfaced both), D-423, D-360._


## 2026-08-08 — D-439 · FIX — Task 265: the cron starvation was our own catch-up call re-stamping Task Scheduler's hostile defaults

**The fix (design §8.6.5).** Every task `register-crons` created carried `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries: true`, `StopIfGoingOnBatteries: true`, `StopOnIdleEnd: true`, `RestartOnIdle: false` — never starts unplugged, dies on unplug, killed the moment the user returns to the keyboard, never retried. D-424 had this off the maintainer's machine; re-read live during the task, unchanged. Registrations now pass `-AllowStartIfOnBatteries -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries -DontStopOnIdleEnd` alongside 167.E/203's `-StartWhenAvailable -WakeToRun`.

**The part D-424 did not have: the kit was doing it to itself.** `Set-ScheduledTask -Settings` REPLACES a task's entire settings object, and `New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet`'s no-argument defaults ARE the hostile posture (verified in-memory on PowerShell 5.1, registering nothing). So the best-effort PowerShell call added by Task 167.E — the one whose job was to make missed runs catch up — re-stamped the bad flags on every single registration. The catch-up fix and the starvation cause were the same line of code.

**Two corrections to D-424's diagnosis, preserved not dropped.** (a) The "10-minute idle REQUIREMENT before it may start" reading is wrong: `RunOnlyIfIdle` is absent from the captured task (schema default `false`) so it never required idle to start, and Microsoft documents `IdleSettings/Duration` + `WaitTimeout` as **deprecated, "no longer used."** (b) Whether `StopOnIdleEnd` fires at all when `RunOnlyIfIdle` is false is **not settled by the primary source** — the docs state it unconditionally and never condition it, while the Task Scheduler UI greys the checkbox. Set explicitly to `false` to remove the question rather than reason about it. The battery pair needs no such hedging and was the load-bearing half.

**DECISION — extend the proven `/Create` + settings-call shape, do NOT move to `schtasks /Create /XML`.** The XML route would set everything atomically and drop the PowerShell dependency, but means hand-authoring Principals / Triggers / Actions on a surface that cannot be live-tested here (a real registration is the user's step), and a malformed XML fails registration outright instead of degrading. **DECISION — `StopOnIdleEnd: false`, not the entry's `RestartOnIdle: true` alternative:** the docs require BOTH true for terminate-and-restart, and not being killed beats being killed and restarted from zero.

**Migration is re-registration, and its outcome is no longer swallowed.** `/Create /F` plus the unconditional settings call means `cmk register-crons` repairs an existing bad registration — no upgrade hook, no separate command. The result now carries `settingsApplied` and the CLI warns when the repair fails, because a registered-but-starving task reported as clean is the same false-green class as D-298's fresh-heartbeat/stale-output.

**Detection deliberately NOT built here.** `inspectWindowsTaskSettings(xml)` ships pure and unwired — `{verdict: 'ok'|'needs-repair'|'unreadable', problems}`, schema-default-aware because schtasks omits elements equal to the default and three of those defaults are the hostile values. **Task 47** already reads the same `schtasks /query /XML` for its registered-target-exists check; it reads once and asks both questions. `unreadable` exists so a check that cannot tell SKIPs rather than FAILs.

**Detour fix, found while wiring `--dry-run`:** the win32 branch wrote the Task-215 VBS shim BEFORE the dry-run return, so `cmk register-crons --dry-run` created `context/.locks/` + a `.vbs` in the user's repo — contradicting the "inspect before granting host permissions" affordance design §8.6.2 documents and HEALTH-CHECKS.md advertises. Path is now computed for display, written only for real.

**Cross-platform check (the entry required it): done, nothing to change.** cron has no battery/idle conditions; `launchd.plist(5)` documents no power- or activity-conditional keys at all and `StartCalendarInterval` already coalesces sleep-missed runs to next wake. Both pinned by non-regression tests so the Windows machinery cannot leak into the POSIX legs.

**Standing caveat, unchanged:** this makes the cron much less bad, not dependable. A scheduler is best-effort; the lazy roll (167.A/D) and the resumable distill (ADR-0020 / Task 204) remain the guarantees.

_Relates D-298 (the starvation), D-424 (the evidence + the two corrected readings), D-207/Task 167.E + Task 203 (the call this extends — and the one that caused it), Task 47 (the detection consumer), Task 215 (the shim), ADR-0020, design §8.6.5, Task 265._

## 2026-08-08 — D-438 · NOTE — The wedge gate is a coin flip, and 262's A/B proved it the hard way

**Finding:** live-verify's Phase A wedge gate fails ~50% of clean serial runs on the same missing rule ("run ruff before committing") — measured across five samples during 262's live-verify attribution, failing and passing in BOTH flag conditions, with a mechanical unreachability proof excluding the linking change entirely (zero fact files in the wedge sandboxes; the flag's single call site requires a fact create). The gate's verdict on any branch is therefore noise on this phase: a ~50% false-alarm rate, and a real capture regression would hide inside it. **Filed as Task 279 (v0.7)** with the fix classes enumerated (prompt-hardening / presence-relaxation / logged-retry / deterministic-fake-agent variant) and a measurable done-bar (<5% false alarms over ≥10 runs, with a seeded regression still caught). The meta-point worth the entry: the five-sample matrix exists because the first sample looked like a clean ON-fails/OFF-passes split — one more sample would have indicted the wrong suspect. Attribution needs samples in BOTH conditions, and the implementer kept sampling past the convenient answer.


## 2026-08-08 — D-437 · BUG/FIX — The test suite ran `cmk autolink` against the maintainer's REAL memory and rewrote 177 fact files

**What happened.** `tests/cli-scaffold.test.js` smoke-runs **every leaf CLI verb bare from the repo cwd** to prove none is still a stub. A newly-registered verb that is not listed in its `NON_STUB_VERBS` set gets executed for real. `cmk autolink` shipped without that entry, so the first full-suite run after registering it executed the verb against **this repo's own 2,260-fact corpus** and added `related:` frontmatter to **177 committed fact files**, plus 177 `auto-linked` entries in the (gitignored) audit log.

**Accounted for before calling it anything (D-366).** Nothing was lost and nothing was moved: linking only ADDS a `related:` key, and a `git diff -U0` over the whole tier showed **every** hunk was a single `+related: [...]` line — the only other changes were `INDEX.md`/`MAP.md`, already modified before this session. The count reconciles exactly: 90 facts carried `related:` before, 267 after, 177 audit entries. All 177 were uncommitted, and the session-start `git status` proved no fact file had prior uncommitted changes, so `git checkout --` on exactly those 177 paths restored them losslessly. **Verified after: 90 again.** The audit-log entries are left in place — the log is append-only and this is honest history of what ran. The repo's `.index/` gained a `link_eval` table and a stored floor: rebuildable derived state, cleared by the next `reindex --full`.

**Two fixes, deliberately at different levels:**
1. **Local** — `autolink` added to `NON_STUB_VERBS` with the reason spelled out, joining `view`, `import-sessions`, `purge` and `redact`, all excluded for the same "must not run unguarded from the repo cwd" reason. That list is now four entries long for one recurring hazard, which is itself the signal.
2. **Structural, and the one that matters** — **bare `cmk autolink` is now a DRY RUN; writing takes an explicit `--apply`.** The test exclusion protects this repo; the flag protects every user. A command that rewrites part of someone's memory must not be what you get from typing its name with no arguments, and "the smoke test will run it" is only the way we happened to find that out. Pinned by test G9, which asserts the bare invocation changes nothing and prints the `--apply` hint.

**The lesson worth keeping:** the failing test was not noise to be silenced by adding an exclusion — it was the guardrail firing, and reading it as "add the verb to the list" would have shipped the real hazard. When a generic test fails on a NEW verb, ask what the test was protecting before you tell it about the exception. _Relates Task 262, D-192/D-193 (memory-delete discipline), D-366 (account for records before naming the loss)._

## 2026-08-08 — D-436 · DECISION — Write-time linking ships DEFAULT-OFF: measured, it REGRESSES the type it was built for

**Task 262 sub-tasks 2-4 are built, tested and live-verified — and the AFTER measurement says the mechanism does not work for its stated purpose on the benchmark that was built to judge it.** Filing this as the finding rather than burying it in a task annotation, because the honest reading changes what should ship.

**The numbers, RESTATED 2026-08-08 after review finding B1** ([research note](../research/2026-08-08-linking-benchmark-baseline.md) §7, same fixture / script / build as the D-433 baseline, fresh and aged agreeing). The first version of this entry collapsed two automatic variants into one number and leaned on a delta the instrument cannot resolve. Corrected, on `graph-hybrid` multi-hop R@5 (the kit's real default recall):

| variant | R@5 | vs unlinked 0.444 | vs ceiling 0.889 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `auto-write` (capture time) | **0.444** | no change | recovers 0% |
| `auto-backfill` (whole corpus) | **0.333** | one question down | recovers 0% |

**What survives the correction:** the BACKFILL — the only arm with comparable edge density to the hand-placed set (19 edges / 14 facts vs 13 / 11) — **recovers 0% of the available gain** and costs the temporal control **0.25**. That is the solid claim, and D-433's condition fires on it.

**What was withdrawn:** the "−0.111 regression" attributed to the write path. One multi-hop question is worth 0.111 — the instrument's resolution floor, which this project's own canary rationale calls single-question wobble. And the write path placed **4 edges over 3 of 50 facts**: structurally starved (it links backward only, and no floor exists below 24 facts), i.e. **effectively unmeasured**, not measured-and-bad.

**Why, and it is not a threshold to tune.** The fixture's multi-hop answers share no distinctive term with their query — by construction the ground-truth edge joins two facts that are topically related and lexically/distributionally far apart. A similarity-ranked linker selects on precisely the axis those edges are weak on. Lowering the floor buys more edges of the same kind, and more edges of that kind is what already made recall worse. The deterministic linker measures the wrong quantity; it is not mis-calibrated.

**What IS true of it.** On the real 2,260-fact corpus the derived floor is 0.1564 (median 0.0724, max 0.3636 over 20,000 pairs — non-degenerate), a live `cmk remember` auto-linked to two edges a human would place, and the dry-run proposes 4,902 edges over 1,843 of 2,170 unlinked facts with a similar character. So the mechanism produces *defensible* links on real data; it does not produce the *specific* links that answer multi-hop questions. Both are true.

**What this settles for ADR-0023.** Its DEFER ranked LLM edge derivation (Memora-style `cues:`) first in line. Sub-task 1 fired the trigger; sub-task 4 now adds the other half — **the cheap deterministic alternative was tried, built, and does not close the gap.** The measurement points back at the ADR's own first-ranked candidate, for the reason the ADR gave.

**DECIDED — default-OFF (lead-ratified 2026-08-08; re-ratified the same day on the corrected basis below).**

The original ratification cited two grounds. Review finding B1 dissolved the first one **as applied to the write path**: the recall regression is the BACKFILL's, and the write path's own arm is unmeasured on this fixture. Re-adjudicated honestly, the decision **stands on the second ground alone**, which is untouched by the correction and is about the write path specifically:

1. ~~It regresses the qtype it was built for~~ — **withdrawn as a reason to default the WRITE PATH off.** It is a real finding about the similarity-ranked edge source (see the numbers above), and it is why ADR-0023's deferred candidate is still the live one; it is not evidence about capture-time placement, which this fixture cannot measure.
2. **It costs unbudgeted write-path latency** — the sole surviving ground, and sufficient on its own: — `cmk remember` 11.6 s → 14.4 s on the real 2,260-fact corpus, **+2.8 s per capture**, growing linearly with corpus size, on the same synchronous path the detached auto-extract child runs under the §8.5 hook ceiling. That composition is **unverified**, which is its own reason not to switch it on for everyone.

**What ships:** the mechanism, the flag, the derived floor and the backfill, all complete. `cmk autolink` (dry-run by default, `--apply` to write) is the deliberate opt-in and needs no configuration; `memory.link_facts: true` / `CMK_LINK_FACTS=1` turns the write path on for anyone who wants it. Flipping the default back is one line in `linkingEnabled`, pinned by test C1 so that flip always breaks a test.

**The user may overrule at the morning review** — this is the lead's call on the implementer's evidence, recorded as decided rather than left hanging. _Relates D-433, ADR-0023, Task 262, Task 143._

## 2026-08-08 — D-435 · BUG/FIX — Two silent composition defects in the linker, both invisible to unit tests, both found only by running the measurement

**Neither would have failed a suite, and both produce confident wrong output rather than an error** — the class this project keeps re-learning.

**(1) A single prepared semantic scorer is wrong for a backfill.** `prepareSemanticSimilarity` (Task 143) embeds ONE incoming text and returns a closure over that vector; its `similarityFn(a, b)` **ignores `a`**. That is exactly right for a capture (one new text vs many candidates) and catastrophic for a backfill, where a single prepared scorer compares every fact in the corpus against whatever probe string happened to prepare it — producing real, plausibly-distributed, meaningless numbers. **Fix:** `makeCachedCosineBackend` — symmetric, both sides read from the content-addressed cache, **zero model calls** (both facts are already embedded; that is what the semantic index IS). Pinned by a test asserting the score depends on both arguments and is symmetric.

**(2) The backfill scored the FILE body while the embedding cache is keyed on the INDEXED body.** The indexer trims; a fact file carries writeFact's surrounding newlines. So every cache lookup missed, every pair silently degraded to token-Jaccard, and those lexical scores were judged against a **semantic** floor of 0.68 — `cmk autolink --semantic` produced exactly **zero** links while reporting a healthy floor and a healthy-looking run. Separately-correct-jointly-broken: the key derivation was right, the scorer was right, the body was right, the composition was silent. **Fix:** score the indexed body; the file body stays the content-sha for edit detection.

**The lesson worth keeping:** the AFTER measurement was not a formality after the tests went green — it was the only instrument that could see either bug. A "0 links" run with a plausible floor looks identical to "nothing qualified". _Relates Task 262, Task 143, D-421 (same keying-assumption family), CLAUDE.md "Composition verification"._

## 2026-08-08 — D-434 · DECISION — Write-time linking: the shape that shipped (Task 262 sub-tasks 2 + 3)

Implementation decisions taken during the build that the grill did not settle, recorded so they are not re-derived:

- **The linker attaches inside `writeFact`**, the one boundary every fact create flows through, and is **sync**. The async model work stays in the caller (the Task-143 `prepareNearDupGuard` shape): a prepared `similarityFn` is injected via `linkSimilarity`, and the always-available token-Jaccard path is the default. Consequence: the write path today links **lexically** on every surface including auto-extract; the semantic path is reached via `cmk autolink --semantic` and the injection seam. Wiring an embed into the explicit-capture adapters is a budget question left for the lead.
- **`related:` stores SLUGS, not ids** — the existing corpus convention (`relatedSlugs`, `rebuildEdges`' slug→id resolution). Verified empirically: 2,260 real facts have **zero** slug collisions after stripping the type prefix.
- **Links never cross tiers.** A committed project fact referencing a machine-local user-tier slug would dangle for every other clone.
- **The backfill never queues near-dups.** Both facts already exist and were both accepted, often months apart; manufacturing hundreds of retroactive merge decisions would bury the conflict queue's real signal. Write time still queues.
- **`link_eval` (the "considered, nothing found" resume artifact) lives in the REBUILDABLE index**, keyed `(id, content_sha)`. Losing it to a full reindex costs a re-consideration — idempotent work, never lost work, and never a second source of truth about the markdown (ADR-0002 / ADR-0020 rule 3).
- **The verb is `cmk autolink`**, not `cmk link` — one character from the existing `cmk links` query verb is a UX trap. CLI-only in the parity guard: the automatic half needs no command, and this is a one-off catch-up the model has no reason to drive mid-conversation.
- **Two DERIVED degeneracy guards** refuse to link when the distribution cannot separate signal from noise (floor ≥ the calibrated near-dup ceiling; floor ≤ the random-pair median). Confirmed live: a sandbox of 30 near-identical facts derives floor 0.90 against a 0.5 ceiling and correctly links nothing.
- **`??=` is not available** to the repo's test transform — it parses under `node --check` and fails under vitest/esbuild with a locationless `SyntaxError`. Worth knowing before the next hour is spent on it. _Relates Task 262, ADR-0020, ADR-0023, D-433._

## 2026-08-08 — D-432 · DECISION — The viewer goes DARK-DEFAULT (light stays supported) — revisiting D-425's ratified direction with new evidence

**This revisits SETTLED decision D-425 (and through it the [2026-08-04 direction memo](../research/2026-08-04-viewer-visual-direction-memo.md)), which is a frozen record and stays untouched.** The memo's ratified position was *"an instrument panel over a warm archive"* — warm LIGHT for everything you read, exactly one permanently dark saturated region (the graph) as the hero. It explicitly rejected dark-first on three grounds: the dullness is polarity-invariant, this is a reading surface, and dark-first makes us a Linear clone.

**The new evidence** — three consecutive dark-preference verdicts, which is the same "third time is data, not preference" standard D-425 itself was filed under: (1) the user's *"still ugly"* on shipped warm-light v0.6.5; (2) *"mnemory screenshots look better"* — a dark reference product, named unprompted; (3) the user commissioning a dark visual pass outright. A memo written before those three is a dated snapshot, not a standing fact. **The user's call 2026-08-08, going with the lead's recommendation: dark becomes the DEFAULT, light stays fully supported.**

**The position, revised:** the product lives beside a terminal and dies with it (Ctrl-C), so it is built out of the same material — warm near-black under warm bone ink, and **exactly ONE accent (amber)** drawn from the ground's own hue family, replacing the cold violet that was a second colour system laid over a warm page. The graph is still the hero, by a different mechanism: **deeper than the page** (`--g-canvas: #131110` under `--ground: #1a1714`) and holding the only dense saturation in the product, rather than being the only dark thing on it.

**PORT, not swap — and this is the load-bearing part.** The dark reference file was generated from the PRE-review page, so dropping it in would have regressed five defects this branch had already fixed. All five are preserved: (1) the **viewport-sized instrument** (`calc(100dvh - 244px)` with the `vh` fallback, the 440px floor, and the `:has([data-state=warn|bad])` 289px override gated above the stacked breakpoint) — the reference still carries the fixed 620px box that put the hero below the fold; (2) the **honest-totals JS** (`readTotal` reads `data.total` only) — the reference still carries the old `ensureTotal`/`corpusTotal` with its `/api/graph` fallback; (3) the **font contract** (§24.1.2: `system-ui` leads, `"Segoe UI Variable"` named nowhere) — the reference names the Variable families three times; (4) the **peek-before-legend insert** and the `#rail-key` id; (5) the **rail with no `max-height`**, so its legend is not behind an internal scrollbar. Nothing from the reference's `<script>` was ported — the two script blocks are byte-identical to this branch's, verified by diff.

**Two reference tokens were ADJUSTED rather than adopted, both because they fail a written contract** (the rule being: a failing reference token gets fixed, never the assertion deleted). `--ink-3` #9d9384 → **#a59b8c**: the reference value computes to **4.23:1** for 11px/600 badge text on its own tint over `--panel`, the one AA failure in the skin. `--sunken` #15120f → **#1e1b17**: the reference put sunken BELOW ground, which breaks §24.1.2's ORDERED-ladder rule (in dark: panel > sunken > ground) and its test.

**Rejected: dark-ONLY.** It was the tempting simplification (the reference file is single-polarity, `color-scheme: dark`, one token block) and it buys nothing functional — it would delete the theme-aware contract, roughly half the AA surface the contrast test computes, and the choice from every user whose eyes or room disagree with ours. Light is now the supported second theme rather than the identity; each theme is served on two signals (the system preference AND an explicit `data-theme`), with a new drift guard pinning the duplicate palettes to each other so a `data-theme` copy cannot ship an unmeasured palette.

**Also rejected: keeping hue-coded tier glyphs in dark.** With one accent that doubles as `--warn`, `--tier-l: var(--accent)` becomes a three-way collision and `--tier-u`'s orange sits next to it; the alternatives were a stray cold violet (the exact thing the revision removes) or monochrome. Dark's tier glyphs are monochrome and the LETTER carries the meaning; **light keeps its hue-coded tiers**, because light's accent is not also its warn hue. This is a deliberate asymmetry, not drift.

## 2026-08-08 — D-433 · NOTE — 262's benchmark fired ADR-0023's trigger before the mechanism exists, found traversal DILUTION, and measured the keyword floor (Task 278)

**Sub-task 1 shipped and the trigger is MET, measured not argued:** the kit's default flat pipeline answers 1.000 of flat-answerable questions and **0.444** of relational ones; hand-placed `related:` edges lift the relational half to 0.889 with no flat loss. The canary discipline (KiroCrew-derived, D-429) held: margin 0.444 against a 0.300 floor on a byte-identical unlinked twin, three controls proving attribution — and the CLI refuses to print a baseline when the canary fails, so the instrument cannot silently measure noise. **The design-shaping finding: link traversal DILUTES when seeded badly** — correct edges cost 0.25 on the temporal control because a junk keyword sub-query made a weak fact the rank-0 seed and its neighbours evicted the right answer; zero cost when seeded from hybrid. The linking mechanism inherits this as a constraint: relational expansion seeds from hybrid, never the keyword ladder alone, and D-226's post-retrieval filtering is now measurable rather than speculative. **The lead ratified an assertion change** (controls now pin "no control may GAIN" + a banded dilution cost, replacing a delta===0 the design never promised — the contract was wrong, not the result; the temporal control also grew 2→4 questions first because a 2-item control cannot resolve anything). **And the benchmark measured something nobody asked it to:** one-shot keyword search — the DEFAULT `cmk search` mode — answers **0 of 21** natural-language questions (implicit-AND; live-verified). The 0.941 headline was always the hybrid pipeline. Filed as **Task 278** (v0.7) with the benchmark's controls as the ready-made judge for any fix.

## 2026-08-07 — D-430 · NOTE — The health whisper's FIRST production firing — it worked exactly as designed, and the cause is a pattern worth naming

**What happened.** Mid-session, the Task-250 whisper surfaced `extract-failing` ("auto-extract keeps failing; turns are not being captured") in the prompt context. Diagnosis from `health.log`: two `haiku_timeout` failures (19:21, 19:31) on the detached extract child's `claude --print` call — landing EXACTLY in the window where this session had four research subagents, npm audits, and validators saturating the machine and the API concurrently. The 90s inner ceiling (itself a live-test finding, 2026-06-01) was not enough under that load. The Task-242 fallback degraded both captures to PARTIAL rather than zero, `now.md` stayed current, and the class clears on the next successful extract.

**Why it gets an entry.** (1) **The whisper validated in production**: two-strike gate (no nag on the single 19:08-adjacent blip), correct class, correct fix-hint, first real firing since shipping 3 days ago — the D-412 design behaving as specced on its first live defect. (2) **The named pattern: self-inflicted load starvation** — the kit's host machine running a heavy agent fan-out starves the kit's own detached extraction; the failure is transient, environmental, and NOT a code regression. The tell that separates it from a real regression: failures correlate with the operator's own burst activity, and the serial/quiet-machine case succeeds (the D-366 falsify-first shape, applied before touching any code). No action beyond diagnosis; if the class ever fires on a QUIET machine, that is the real-problem branch.

## 2026-08-06 — D-427 · NOTE + DECISION — The 268 review pass: two blocking findings fixed, the font contract enforced over the disclaimer, and a write-path durability bug found by accident (Task 270)

**The two-pass discipline earned its keep on the redesign.** The reviewer's holistic pass over `task-268-viewer-redesign` returned 2 Blocking / 6 Important / 11 Minor, all traced with file:line — plus the process catch that the live-verify fix was verified but never committed (it sat in the working tree while the branch was reviewed; a merge would have silently dropped it). The blockers: **(B1)** the fold fix's `244px` chrome subtraction was calibrated against the OK-state health strip only — in warn/bad the strip deliberately goes loud (+~45px, and asynchronously, a beat after first paint), handing the graph view its scrollbar back on exactly the day something is wrong; fixed with a `:has()` state-aware override, gated above the stacked breakpoint. **(B2)** the integration had recorded the Claude-Design font stack (`Segoe UI Variable Text/Display` first) as an *"OPEN DISAGREEMENT pending the maintainer's ratification"* — while DELETING the test that enforced design §24.1.2's explicit "NOT Segoe UI Variable" line. The lead's call, recorded here: **the contract wins; the disclaimer was the lazy-framing class** (green-and-disclaimed, the exact shape CLAUDE.md's anti-pattern list names as a merge blocker). Stack reverted, guard restored and anchored to the `--ui:` declaration. Any future font change goes contract-first: design.md flips in the same commit as the code and the test.

**The Important tier was population bugs, not paint.** The redesign's 56px hero figure counted a **different population** than the list beneath it (`/api/graph`'s expiry-blind `fact_count` vs `/api/facts`' filtered rows) at the cost of a full graph computation per facts page-load, and in search mode showed the page-capped `count` as "N matches" (a 500-hit query rendered "50 matches" in the largest type on screen). Fixed by pulling **Task 269 item (2)** forward: `/api/facts` now returns `total` in both modes — with the count living in `search.mjs` (`countKeywordMatches`, sharing the newly single-owner `keywordFilters` + `applyStateView`), because a viewer-side COUNT of the raw query would AND the Task-211 history hint words into the match and could return a total SMALLER than the page it captions. Also fixed: the AA ratio test now covers the graph's entire `--g-*` colour space (it previously guarded only the archive tokens — the dark region's 11–12.5px text had NO contrast guard; all six pairs pass, worst 4.88:1), and the hover peek now inserts ABOVE the "Reading it" legend (it appended below, so on a rail that just fit, pointing at a node produced feedback below the fold — the second half of the user's original complaint).

**The accidental find is the important one (Task 270, laned v0.6.6).** The implementer's fixture id `P-5678ABCD` (`8` is outside the base32 alphabet) exposed that **`writeFact` accepts an explicitly-supplied invalid id, returns `action: 'created'`, lands a real file — and the index sync then skips it**, making the fact invisible to every DB-backed route forever. No error anywhere; the only symptom was a count off by one. A durable-but-unfindable fact is the D-366 class manufactured by our own write path. The `FACT_ID_REPAIRED` repair (D-394) did NOT fire across two live requests against the orphan — why is unconfirmed and is the task's first sub-task. `validate-test-ids` guards this alphabet in tests only — one layer too late for real callers (imports, restores, MCP clients).


## 2026-08-06 — D-426 · FIX + NOTE — The redesigned graph was below the fold, and the check for it found that 91% of the corpus is unreachable

**The fix.** The Task-268 redesign shipped the graph instrument as a fixed `620px` box beneath ~350px of eyebrow, headline and an explanatory paragraph, so on a 900px laptop **the hero of the Graph view started below the fold** — and the legend rail was pinned at `max-height: 620px`, hiding its own "Reading it" key behind an internal scrollbar. The user's report was exact: *"i want to see the graph in the page, why do i need to go down and why do i need to move it so i can see the Reading it?"* **My error, named:** the design brief asked for a display type tier and a corpus overview, and I let both apply to the *graph* view, where the instrument IS the content — and the paragraph I put above it said the same thing as the rail's legend, so the page spent a screen saying it twice. Fixed: the header is one compact baseline row, the duplicated paragraph is gone, the instrument sizes to the viewport (`dvh` with a `vh` fallback and a 440px floor), the canvas and rail fill it, and the graph view claws back most of `main`'s 88px footer padding so nothing scrolls for a scrollbar's sake. The stacked breakpoint opts out, because there the two are siblings in one column. Test (4b) pins **viewport-relative sizing as the contract** rather than any particular number, and was watched failing on the pre-fix layout before being kept.

**The finding, which is the more important half.** The user's follow-up — *"about the rest of the tabs, like health, can't we do just a few, add pages instead of a long list, and search in all of them?"* — read as a styling question and is not one. Measured on the real corpus: `/api/facts` and `/api/decisions` clamp at `VIEWER_MAX_LIMIT = 200`, and **the viewer has no `offset`, `cursor`, or `page` parameter anywhere** — a grep for all four across `viewer.mjs` returns zero. So **91% of the fact corpus (2,300) and 92% of the decision journal (2,502) cannot be reached through the viewer by any URL.** `/api/decisions` already returns `{count: 200, total: 2502, truncated: true}`: it knows it is hiding 2,302 entries, says so in the envelope, and offers nothing to do about it. The facts envelope does not even carry `total`, so that view structurally cannot state its own scale — the same gap Task 268 item (3) attacks visually, one layer down. Filed as **Task 269**, v0.6.6, riding with 268 as the functional half of the same surface. **Health is explicitly excluded**: 15 checks is a complete list, not a truncated one, and paginating it would be ceremony.

**The pattern worth recording.** This is the third time this session a user *question* — not a failing test — surfaced a real defect (after the aged-corpus index corruption and the stale cron flags). A capability that is silently capped looks identical to a capability that is complete, from inside the test suite: every viewer test asserts on a response that fits under the cap. The live-verify extension owed here is the assertion that would have caught it — **a second page must return different records than the first** — which is the Task 263 ratchet applied to this surface.
