## 2026-08-05 — D-424 · NOTE — Three backlog items filed from what v0.6.5's work surfaced (Tasks 265/266/267), and a correction owed to the user

**v0.6.5 shipped, and three real problems surfaced ALONGSIDE it that were not the release — filed now so they survive the session (the amnesia the kit exists to kill, applied to its own backlog):** **Task 265** — the registered crons carry `StopOnIdleEnd: true` + `RestartOnIdle: false` + battery blocks, read directly off the maintainer's Task Scheduler XML: D-298's five-night starvation is not a mystery, it is these flags (Windows kills the job the moment the user returns, and never retries; a laptop on battery never runs it at all). Every Layer-6 job inherits them. **Task 266** — three dependency-advisory fires in one week (postcss + five transitives), each caught only by turning a feature PR's gate red; we react per-incident instead of on a cadence. **Task 267** — the install opt-ins (semantic, crons) that a new user must know the flags exist to enable.

**The correction, recorded because it is a pattern not a one-off:** Task 267 was very nearly filed on a FALSE premise — the assistant asserted three times this session that the user had NOT run `--with-semantic`/`register-crons`, when the user runs the full stack (semantic index holds 2,321 real embeddings; crons registered and ran last night), a fact ALREADY in memory as `P-4aK276X4` that the assistant recalled past. All three false claims (the cron flags before reading them, "semantic never installed" after checking the wrong dir, "user didn't run the opt-in") came from asserting inferences about the user's environment instead of verifying — the exact "did you check the primary source?" class the project binds, applied to the user rather than to a library. Captured as a durable `user`-tier lesson (`P-WT94TJSD`). Task 267 is filed with the corrected framing: the argument is NOT "users skip the opt-ins" (this user didn't) but the sharper "on-is-not-enough, it can be silently broken" — which is what Task 261 addresses.

## 2026-08-04 — D-423 · FIX + DECISION — Task 261 built: the vec index is keyed by the fact's own id, the repair command now cures what it caused, and a SECOND bug fell out of the red test

**Numbering note.** This entry was written as D-422 and RENUMBERED to D-423 on landing: the lead filed their own D-422 (the fresh-vs-aged live-test audit + Task 263) concurrently on the same branch while this work was in flight. Theirs landed first and is already cited by Task 263, so it keeps the number. Commit messages from this task's build cite `D-422` for content that now lives here — recorded rather than rewritten, since the commits are already made and the trail should show what happened.

**The diagnosis in D-421 held exactly, and the red test proved it before a line of the fix was written.** Querying with a fact's own body returned a *neighbour's* fact at score **1.0** — the corruption is not degraded ranking, it is a clean, confident, wrong answer, which is why nothing ever noticed it. Three reds: the rowid reassignment, a real `reindexFull` over a real project tier, and one the filing did not predict.

**The unpredicted one, worth its own line.** `present && plan.cached → continue` treats *"this content is in the cache"* as *"this slot holds this content"*, and the two are unrelated. A body edited to text some OTHER fact had already embedded keeps its stale vector **forever** — no rebuild required, no rowid involved. Same unsound-guard family as D-421, independent trigger, found only because writing the invariant down (*every fact recalls itself*) forced the question of what the guard actually proves. Fixed by the same change: `vec_map.content_sha` records what is physically in each slot, so the skip test is an equality that means what it says.

**The design, and why it is not the fix the task specified.** Task 261 (a) asked for a lockstep DROP of the vec tables inside `reindexFull` plus the same on the per-path delete. Built (b) first instead — `vec_map(scope, key1, key2, vec_rowid, content_sha)` keyed on `observations.id` / `(source_file, chunk_idx)`, allocated explicitly, never auto-assigned — and (a) then **dissolves rather than ships**: with the key immune to rebuilds, dropping the vec tables on every full reindex discards work that is free to keep, and the per-path delete needs nothing at all because a per-file re-index never changes a fact's id. **The lead should ratify this substitution**; the alternative (ship both) is a correctness-neutral pessimization kept as a safety net, and I judged a net under a fix that cannot fail to be worth less than the clarity of one mechanism. What (a) *was* protecting — "the documented repair must repair" — is now (c)'s job and better served: `reindexFull` clears the `map_version` marker, which force-rebuilds the vec layer on the next sync. Marker rather than `DROP`, because dropping a `vec0` virtual table **requires sqlite-vec loaded on that connection** (verified empirically: `no such module: vec0` on both `DROP` and `DELETE`) and a plain reindex has no reason to load it.

**Repair is automatic and free, and both halves were asserted rather than assumed.** The same `map_version` generation marker heals every already-corrupted install on its next sync — no command, no notice, nothing for the user to know. The "free" claim is a test assertion, not a comment: a rebuild-then-resync performs **0 embeds** because `embedding_cache` is content-addressed and deliberately never touched. **HC-15** is the never-silent-again half: it samples live facts, re-derives `sha256(model
body)` from the body on record and byte-compares the cached vector against what is physically in the slot — trusting none of the kit's own bookkeeping, so a corrupted map is caught too. No embedder, no model load.

**One honest limit:** HC-15 cannot see the pre-261 corruption (a pre-261 index has no map to audit), so it SKIPs there. That is acceptable only because such an index self-heals on its next sync — the check exists to guard the NEW scheme, and the OLD one is repaired rather than reported.

**D-421's 15.4% was an UNDERCOUNT, and the corrected number is 86.7%.** Measured directly on the real index at the moment of the fix — for every live fact, does its slot hold its own vector? — **2,012 of 2,321 (86.7%) did not.** D-421 counted *duplicate vectors* (rows sharing a byte-identical vector with another row), which is a PROXY, and a bad one: a permutation of N vectors across N slots produces every fact wrong and **zero** duplicates. Both figures are honest measurements; only one measures the thing that matters. Recording it because the first number was the reassuring one, and a data-integrity figure that understates by 5.6× is the framing class this project has a rule about. AFTER: `cmk reindex --full` then `cmk search "never overwrite backup directories" --mode semantic` returns the backup facts, top hit `P-YPZNXDYR` ("never overwrite or reuse an existing backup dir"), and the same direct measurement reports **2,323 of 2,323 correct, 0 wrong**. `cmk doctor` → 15 pass · 0 fail, HC-15 PASS in 487 ms.

**THE AGED-CORPUS HARNESS — the deliverable grew, because the bug was a symptom of a testing gap (the user's call, mid-task).** We audited why this survived and the answer was uncomfortable: the cut-gate DOES live-test semantic recall with real paraphrase queries and zero keyword overlap, and it was passing **legitimately**. So do all four live-verify scripts. Every one of them shares one shape — `mkdtemp` → `cmk install` → a handful of facts → ONE index build → assert. On a first build every derived table is numbered in insert order and agrees with its source **by luck rather than by contract**. **We verified fresh state and never aged state.** That is a class, not a bug.

**[`scripts/lib/aged-corpus.mjs`](../../scripts/lib/aged-corpus.mjs) is the answer, built as a REUSABLE seam** (design §17.14): `build → MUTATE → rebuild → assert`, where the mutation phase drives the REAL `cmk` bin through what actually happens to a corpus over months — facts written, one expired, one forgotten, one superseded, a fact file removed by hand, then `reindex --boot` AND `reindex --full`, asserting after each. Nothing in the harness is about vectors; the caller supplies assertions through `afterCycle`, so the next task with a "true only because the index was just built" assumption adds a check rather than forking another fresh-corpus script. Wired at 2 cycles into the ALWAYS-ON suite (`tests/cli-aged-corpus.test.js`, ~10 s) and at 3 cycles into a new `npm run live-verify:semantic` — which also closes a second gap nobody had noticed: **semantic search had NO automated live coverage at all**, only the manual cut-gate.

**Several cycles, not one — and I have the receipt.** Against the pre-fix build the aged run survived cycle 1 and failed on **cycle 2's full rebuild**, returning `P-ZGYXEP9W` ("Tombstones live under…") at score **1.0** for a query that was the verbatim body of `P-TE7WT2AC` ("The snapshot builder must not import the sqlite index module"). The FRESH baseline in the same run PASSED. A one-cycle harness would have reported green — the defect needs a rebuild over an already-populated index, which one cycle cannot produce.

**Stubbed embedder, stated rather than glossed:** the aging is 100% real bins, because that is the half that reassigns row numbers; the EMBEDDING is stubbed through the existing `extractorImpl` DI seam, because the invariant is a MAPPING property (does slot X hold fact X's vector?) that holds or fails independent of what vectors mean — and gating it on a 110 MB model would make it skippable, which is precisely how this class shipped green. **A production env-var fake-embedder was considered and REJECTED:** a flag that writes meaningless vectors into a content-addressed durable cache is a footgun aimed at real memory. Real meaning gets its own opt-in pass (`CMK_LIVE_EMBEDDER=1`, real bin, paraphrase query).

**The harness found two more bugs on its first real run, both the same family — derived state that outlives or forward-references the table it describes.**

1. **`cmk reindex --full` ABORTS on any corpus containing a supersession.** `observations.superseded_by REFERENCES observations(id)`, and better-sqlite3 turns FK enforcement ON by default (raw SQLite defaults it off). The indexer commits ONE SOURCE FILE per transaction, so a fact whose `superseded_by` points at a fact walked LATER violates the constraint at insert time and the whole rebuild dies with an unhandled `SqliteError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`, leaving the index half-built — on the incremental path too. Two legal shapes hit it and neither is user error: a FORWARD reference (pure filename-sort luck) and a DANGLING one (the successor was later forgotten). **The constraint was buying nothing**: referential integrity here is owned by the markdown (ADR-0002), there is no `ON DELETE` action, and every reader already tolerates an unresolvable target (`graph-index` marks `dst_resolved=0`). Its only observable effect was turning a legitimate corpus into a crash of the documented repair command. FK enforcement is now explicitly OFF on the derived index. **Why nobody hit it: the dogfood corpus has ZERO supersessions**, so the question had never been asked — verified, not assumed.
2. **`meta.edges_built_at` outlives the `edges` table it describes.** `reindexFull` DROPs `edges` but not `meta`, and re-applies the schema with `CREATE IF NOT EXISTS`, so the sentinel survives. The edge rebuild that follows is best-effort inside a `try/catch` — so a throw leaves an EMPTY edges table beside a sentinel claiming it was built, and `reindexBoot`'s cold-edge path (`!edgesBuilt(db)`) then never rebuilds it: `cmk links`, backlinks and supersession chains return empty, confidently, until some unrelated file happens to change. `migrateEdgesSchema` already pairs the drop and the clear, and says why in its own comment; `reindexFull` was violating the invariant its sibling was written to maintain. Now paired, and cleared BEFORE the rebuild so only a rebuild that SUCCEEDED writes the sentinel.

Both were found by the harness, not by review — which is the argument for the harness.

**A test-seam change came first, deliberately:** `prepareSemanticBackend` gained the `extractorImpl` seam `syncSemanticIndex` already had. Without it this gate would skip whenever the optional 110 MB embedder is absent — and a skippable test is not a gate for a class that shipped 15% wrong vectors while the suite was green.

## 2026-08-03 — D-420 · DECISION — Task 260 built: the visual pass is skin-only, and `queued` keeps the quiet shape

**All eight ranked changes landed in `viewer-page.html` alone** — the diff is CSS plus the rendering shape of five view functions; no route, no fetch, no payload field, no state semantics moved (verified by diffing the inline script against `def9f4b7` with comments stripped: `api` / `paintStrip` / `repaintStripFrom` / `currentRoute` / `show` / `go` / `factsSeq` / `copyRow` are byte-identical). `package.json` untouched, `live-verify:viewer` 25/25 unchanged, the anti-XSS sink scan still passes. **The values are the survey's, not taste** — claude-mem's warm ink `#2b2520` and dark ground `#1a1916`, PAI's tint formula, Radix slate's step semantics — recorded as a keepable contract in **design §24.1.2** so the next editor inherits rules rather than a diff. **13 structural tests** were added first and watched fail; two of the three initial reds were my own CSS *comments* tripping my own "this shape must not appear" scans (a comment saying `border-bottom: none` reads identically to the declaration), and one was a genuinely-wrong test regex — `'<b>'` is the needle the FTS marker is *split on*, not assembled markup — narrowed to catch concatenation instead, and called out rather than silently loosened.

**One judgment call the lead should ratify.** The task said the health strip becomes "a full-width bar ONLY when not-ok", and `queued` is literally not-`ok` — but on §24.1.1's own precedence ladder `queued` sits **one rung above all-clear** (pending queue items, below every doctor and health-log rung), and the maintainer's live corpus has 3 review items, so the literal reading gave the second-quietest state the loudest shape and reproduced the exact bug being fixed in a new colour. Shipped as **pill for `ok` and `queued`, bar for `warn` and `bad`**; the alternative is one CSS selector.

**Two things the pass could NOT fix and did not pretend to.** (1) A flattened bullet list still reads as `- A - B - C`. **Corrected after review — the first version of this entry scoped that to "a snippet body", and the real blast radius is larger: measured over 200 facts, 48 snippets AND 15 TITLES carry a flattened bullet run**, so the defect reaches the card's most prominent line, not just its second. A related shape from the same cause: **8 of 200 titles are under 20 characters** and orphan their actual value into the body (`"What changed:"` as a title is correct but carries no information). The flattening is the SERVER's (`flatten(rich.headline, 240)`), and changing it is a payload change this pass was forbidden — it is follow-up material, and the honest statement is that titles are affected too. (2) A few graph labels still overlap where the hand-rolled force layout parks two hubs close together; truncation to 32 code points plus `paint-order` halos reduced it, and a real fix is collision-aware label placement — a feature, filed as a follow-up on Task 260 rather than smuggled into a CSS pass.

**Fix round (2026-08-03, post-review).** One Blocking, four Important, four Minor — all fixed, gates re-run on the final code. The **Blocking** was self-inflicted by the very fix that added the title tier: `factText` hardcoded the list-mode marker strip, so the DETAIL view stripped bullets in its head card while `richBody` printed them two blocks lower on the SAME page; 52 of 120 sampled facts have multi-line headlines and all of them rendered wrong. Root cause was two-part — the mode was not threaded through, AND `TITLE_MIN` suppressed a line-break boundary, so a short first line ("**What changed:**") swallowed its own newline and decapitated the first bullet. A line break now outranks every other boundary and ignores the minimum. **Contrast was arbitrated as fix-don't-document**: 27 of 66 light pairs failed AA, the worst at 2.93:1, and `--ink-3` alone carries the meta row, `.micro`, `#freshness`, `#graph-note`, `.muted`, `.empty`, inactive nav and the READ-ONLY pill; every light hue was darkened and dark's `--ink-3`/`--bad` lifted, with the AA target now written into design §24.1.2 and **computed by a test** (red-then-green verified: reverting one token reports `ink-3 on panel = 3.83:1`). Also: the ratified queued-is-a-pill call is now pinned structurally rather than in prose (the D-169 gap), `DOMParser` joined both sink lists, `--sunken` was reordered genuinely below `--ground`, graph labels slice code points instead of UTF-16 units, and the B1 test runs the page's own render code against a 20-line element shim — real behaviour, still zero dependencies. **Deferred with a named trigger:** the title splitter is polynomial (520 ms at 32 kB, localhost-only today) — revisit if `headline`/`why`/`how` ever gain an untrusted-length path.

## 2026-08-04 — D-422 · DECISION + NOTE — Why Task 261 survived every gate: we live-test FRESH state and never AGED state. Task 263 filed as a standing ratchet.

**The user's question was the right one: "we haven't done live test? how did we not see this?"** The honest audit took two corrections, both of which I got wrong first and the user caught.

**Correction 1 (mine, unprompted):** I checked only `scripts/live-verify*.mjs`, found none touching semantic, and said semantic had *"never been live-tested."* **Wrong.** The user pushed back — *"i am sure that Semantic search was tested, at least in the cut-gates"* — and they were right. Cut-gate stage **W2** is a genuine semantic live test: real `cmk search` with paraphrase queries carrying ZERO keyword overlap, asserting the right fact returns, with the explicit note *"Keyword-only would miss both — that's the 0.176 → 0.941 bench gap shipping."* It runs at every cut and has been passing legitimately. **The lesson about asserting from a partial check landed twice in ten minutes** (the first was quoting a cron flag set I hadn't read); both are recorded because the corrections are the evidence the method works.

**The real finding, which is more useful than my sloppy version:** W2 passes **correctly**. It runs on a FRESH install — one session, a handful of facts, a single index build — where rowids are assigned 1..N in insert order and vectors align perfectly. Semantic recall genuinely works on day one. **Nothing anywhere proves it still works on day two hundred, after N rebuilds** — and 261's bug lives exactly there. All four automated live scripts share the flaw (`mkdtemp` + `cmk install`, all of them). So: **our live testing verifies fresh state and never aged state**, and any bug that only manifests on an aged corpus is invisible BY CONSTRUCTION. Naming it as the sibling of the existing rule: **fresh-green ≠ works-on-an-aged-corpus.**

**The bitter part:** the one real aged corpus we own is this repo — dogfooded, `--with-semantic` installed, months of rebuilds. We had the perfect test subject the whole time and never pointed an assertion at it. And the degradation is invisible to a human user: when a memory search returns mediocre results there is no signal distinguishing *"the index is corrupt"* from *"nothing good matched."* Without an assertion, we would have gone on not noticing indefinitely.

**Decisions:** (1) the **aged-corpus harness** (build → mutate → rebuild → THEN assert, multiple cycles) is folded into Task 261's deliverable as a REUSABLE seam rather than a follow-up — the fix is not verifiable without it, and the red proof must come from the aged path specifically since the fresh path legitimately passes; (2) **semantic joins the automated live set** (it existed only in the manual gate); (3) **Task 263 filed as a standing ratchet** with the measured gap — 98 manual cut-gate stages vs 6 automated scripts, only 2 with real assertions — targeting 12/98 → 60/98 worked down every release, ordered by class-value (aged dimension → derived-state health checks → high-harm stages → honestly marking the human-only ones) rather than by list order. **The cheapest guard is not a test at all:** 261's health check (every stored vector equals its own fact's cached vector) runs in production on real corpora and needs nobody to remember it.

## 2026-08-04 — D-421 · BUG + DECISION — The semantic vector index is keyed by a rowid that reindex reassigns; 15% of facts hold another fact's embedding, and `reindex --full` is the CAUSE. v0.6.5 held to ship with the fix.

**Found sideways, which is the part worth remembering.** The user asked a routine question about Task 262's link cap ("how do other projects do it?"). The outward survey answered it (cap 3, well-evidenced) and then reported something else: spot-checked semantic neighbours were *unrelated* at similarities of 0.83–0.88. Chasing that produced the bug. **The cap question was never the important one — asking it is what found this.**

**Verified three ways before filing (D-366 discipline, and my own first measurement was WRONG):** (1) the kit's own CLI — `cmk search "never overwrite backup directories" --mode semantic` returns five facts about gate tests, DECISIONS.md, hook ceilings, tombstones and release workflow, **none about backups**, while `P-BYPATXDD` exists; (2) direct measurement — 2,332 vec rows, 1,972 distinct vectors, **360 rows (15.4%) sharing a vector with an unrelated fact** (sampled pairs confirmed semantically unrelated, which rules out the innocent content-addressed explanation); (3) falsification — `embedding_cache` has 9,697 rows with 9,697 DISTINCT vectors (embedder fine), and the 2,332 `(chunk_id, chunk_offset)` slots are unique (not a read artifact). **My first pass reported 447 dups + 88 all-zero vectors; that was my own bad mapping** (I assumed sequential packing instead of reading the real `chunk_id`/`chunk_offset` columns). Redone correctly: 360 dups, 0 zeros — matching the independent finding exactly. Recording the wrong number and its correction because the correction is the evidence the method was sound.

**Root cause, traced to the line.** `semantic-backend.mjs:13` documents the assumption as if it were safe — *"The vec table mirrors `observations` rowids"* — but `index-rebuild.mjs:382` inserts observations with no explicit rowid (SQLite auto-assigns), and `reindexFull` (`:682-695`) drops `observations`/`observations_fts`/`transcript_chunks`/`files`/`edges` **but not `vec_observations`**. A rebuild reassigns every rowid while the vec table keeps the old mapping; the skip guard at `semantic-backend.mjs:317` (`present && plan.cached → continue`) then reads the stale row as correct and never replaces it, and `:454` joins `observations o ON o.rowid = m.rowid` to return the wrong fact. **So `cmk reindex --full` — the documented repair the troubleshooting skill prescribes — is the cause, and there is no user-facing repair today.** The bitter detail: `index-rebuild.mjs:374`'s own comment warns about *"silent stale-hit corruption"* and adds an explicit DELETE to prevent it — **for the FTS path**. The vec table has the identical hazard and never got the same treatment. A composition gap between two correct components, exactly the class CLAUDE.md's composition-verification rule exists for.

**Why every gate missed it:** a FRESH index assigns rowids 1..N in insert order, so vectors line up perfectly and the suite is green. Corruption appears only on a rebuild over an EXISTING index — which no test performs and which is precisely how a real corpus evolves over months. The unit-green ≠ works-on-real-input class, landing on the kit's headline recall feature while the README advertises R@5 0.941.

**Decisions:** (1) **Task 261 filed** with the fix shape — drop the vec tables in lockstep (re-population is FREE; the cache is intact and content-addressed), then key by the stable `id` rather than the rowid, plus a repair path for corrupted installs and a cheap HC so it can never be silent again. (2) **Task 262 (write-time linking) is BLOCKED on it** — auto-linking on this surface would have written ~4,000 links, a large fraction wrong, into the tier whose whole value is trustworthiness. (3) **v0.6.5 is HELD, not re-versioned** — the user's call ("hold and ship them together"); it was cut but never tagged, so nothing is public and the fix lands inside 0.6.5. The bug is NOT a 0.6.5 regression (it predates the viewer and README work), so this is a judgment call about what we're willing to publish, not a correctness gate.

**Also surfaced, filed separately:** the registered cron on the maintainer's machine carries `StopOnIdleEnd: true` + `RestartOnIdle: false` + battery blocks — **D-298's five-night starvation, visible in configuration** — and `register-crons` is an optional command `install` never calls, so most users have no maintenance at all.

## 2026-08-03 — D-419 · DECISION — Task 260 filed (the viewer's visual pass, v0.6.5) and Task 259 LANED to v0.6.6 by the user's call

**The user ran `cmk view` and said it plainly: "even just from looking at the first page i can say it is ugly."** Correct, and the gap is in the PROCESS before it is in the CSS — **the 255 grill asked what the viewer should SHOW and never what it should LOOK LIKE**, so "legibility over polish" was a lead's passing call rather than a ratified criterion, and visual quality was never a done-condition. Recorded so the next UI-bearing grill asks the question. The user's follow-up was the right instinct too — *"did we research a project that does something like it? i want to see how they do it"* — so the answer was research, not taste: two surveys, all values read from primary-source CSS, plus **screenshots of our page rendered beside the references** ([note](../research/2026-08-03-viewer-visual-design.md)).

**The finding that matters: the zero-dep constraint was never the problem.** Deno's docs ship **117 KB of CSS with zero drop-shadows**; datasette has exactly one; both look good. Separation is 1px borders plus one background step. Our page is plain for eight specific, cheap reasons — no card title at a ~1040px measure (vs claude-mem's 650), outlined rather than tinted badges, a 2% page-to-panel delta so nothing reads as a surface, no type hierarchy, inverted spacing rhythm (padding 13px, gap 9px), a seam-shaped tab bar, a full-width bar for the GOOD health state, and a graph ringed with ~150 unrelaxed orphan nodes. All eight are inline CSS. **Task 260 filed, v0.6.5** — patch, because a visual pass on a shipped feature is polish by D-24's definition, and v0.7.0 stays reserved for the team layer. Its done-criteria end with the one thing no test can assert: *the user looks at it in a real browser and says it's better.*

**Task 259 (viewer live-refresh) LANED to v0.6.6** on the user's call ("put 259 for next version"), converting its named trigger into a lane — conditional work gets triggers, committed work gets lanes (D-267), and the user committed it. Deliberately laned AFTER 260 so the visual pass, the thing actually noticed, is not held behind a feature nobody has asked for. Original trigger preserved in the entry for the trail.

## 2026-08-03 — D-418 · DECISION — The README's front door gets a word cap: one line per feature, detail to `docs/FEATURES.md`

**The user's catch:** *"the Features section in the readme is getting out of hand."* Measured before acting: **19 bullets averaging 85 words, longest 179, ~1,675 words in the section alone.** Then measured the field rather than opining — datasette **489** words in its ENTIRE README (6 bullets, max 13 words) · uv **1,080** (13 bullets, max 14) · claude-mem, the 89k★ direct competitor, **1,691** (44 bullets, max 20) · turso **2,158** (26 bullets, max 22). **Nobody exceeds ~22 words per bullet, and our Features section alone outweighed three of those four complete READMEs.** The structural convention is also consistent: Quick Start / Installation first, a short feature list, then a Documentation section that links out (uv does exactly the two-tier split the user proposed; claude-mem has no features section at all).

**The cause was our own rule, not carelessness.** The binding "document user-facing capabilities in the same PR" rule (D-17) said only ADD A LINE — so 19 releases of well-written paragraphs accreted into an unreadable wall. Fixed in both halves: the README now carries **12 bullets, ≤19 words each, 231 words total** plus a `Full feature detail →` link; every word of the old prose is preserved in the new [`docs/FEATURES.md`](../FEATURES.md), organized under headings (nothing lost — the decision-trail rule applies to docs too). D-17 itself is amended with the brevity half, and per "prose rules vs enforcement" the cap is now **structural**: a new `brevity` family in `validate-docs` fails the suite on any Features bullet over 25 words or a missing detail-doc link (red-then-green tested; the cap sits deliberately ABOVE the field's 22-word max so it catches the accretion class, not a well-judged sentence). Without the validator the same rule would rebuild the same wall over the next 19 releases.

## 2026-08-02 — D-417 · DECISION + NOTE — Task 255 SHIPPED (PR #335): the viewer is live; the review's Blocking was another false-green; and the REMOTE gates caught what no local suite could

**Shipped through the full two-pass split.** Implementer: 9 build commits + 7 self-review findings, plus a gate catch worth its own line — the scaffold smoke-test ran bare `cmk view` as a subprocess, which **bound a port, opened a browser over the maintainer's real memory, and silently wedged two full-suite runs**; fixed both specifically (`NON_STUB_VERBS`) and generically (a 60s `runCmk` timeout the harness never had, so the next blocking verb fails by name instead of hanging for 20 minutes). Independent review: **1 Blocking + 3 Important + 8 Minor**, every one reproduced against the real bin — and the security core HELD (no XSS by full sink enumeration plus a real seeded attack payload; the 81-combination read-only matrix complete; loopback, Host-guard and traversal all clean). **B1 was the same false-green class as 250's, one level up:** the pinned health strip claimed "capture, recall and the index are all reporting fine" directly above a red `cmk doctor` HC-1 FAIL, because health-LOG silence is not health — an unregistered hook never runs, so it never logs. The strip now folds the doctor table on the full route and the cheap `?strip=1` path claims only what it can prove; the live-verify check that should have caught it was tautological (comparing `activeWarnings` to HC-14, which IS `activeWarnings`) and is now a real broken-hooks repro.

**The remote-gates round (the transferable lesson).** With every local gate green — 3903 tests, stress 5/5, live-verify 25/25 — the PR still failed on **CodeQL (3 alerts: 2 high) and SonarCloud (C reliability)**, neither of which the local suite can run. All four were real: the 500 handler returned exception text to the browser (`js/stack-trace-exposure` — now a constant, detail to the terminal only); the anti-XSS sink-scan regexes missed uppercase `<SCRIPT>` and then, in round 2, legal end-tag junk `</script\t\n bar>` (`js/bad-tag-filter` ×2 — **the XSS guard held to its own standard, twice**); and the search input + tier select had no accessibility labels (the two BUGs driving the reliability rating). Two Sonar-flagged super-linear regexes in `rich-fact.mjs` were fixed alongside as genuine ReDoS-adjacent risk on LLM-written bodies (marker-only matching + plain-code slicing — linear, and the nested ternaries fell out with them). **The rule this reinforces:** a green local suite is not a green PR, exactly as a green workflow is not a green commit (D-378) — the app-check gates speak only on the remote, and they must be watched by name.

**One transient recorded, not dismissed:** the first live-verify run after a 20-minute 5×stress ended in `read ECONNRESET`; the identical code passed 25/25 standalone immediately after. Not reproducible, ran under exceptional machine load, at a subprocess-spawn point. By Task 250's own noise threshold a single self-recovering transient is non-actionable — but a SECOND `ECONNRESET` from `live-verify:viewer` is a named signal to investigate socket lifetime in the server rather than the environment.

## 2026-08-02 — D-416 · DECISION — Task 258 REJECTED on its own precondition: the stale-refs scan has no signal on a reference-dense corpus

**The precondition did its job.** Task 258 (the awrshift borrow — D-410's one axis where a shape-class peer led us) shipped with an explicit gate: measure the false-positive rate on OUR corpus before wiring anything. Its trigger fired when Task 250 shipped; the measurement ran ([note](../research/2026-08-02-stale-refs-noise-floor.md), 2,160 fact files): **49.1% of path-shaped references fail to resolve naively; 39.1% even with the skip-list a real implementation would ship** — the skip-list halves the volume and barely moves the rate, because the residue is not a class a skip-list reaches. Hand-classifying all 43 surviving tokens: ~20 are slash-as-"or" PROSE (`MEMORY.md/SOUL.md`, `HABITS/LESSONS/USER.md` — English, not paths, and unfixable by a path scanner given how we write), ~12 are other repos' files from research notes (`letta/constants.py`, `storage/store.rs`), ~8 are tier-relative shorthand, and exactly **2 are genuinely gone — both correct to be gone** (`validate-references.mjs`/`validate-doc-registry.mjs`, consolidated by Task 186). **Honest yield: 2 benign hits per 151 checked at a 39% FP rate**, which fails D-412's ratified actionability bar by an order of magnitude — wiring it would degrade the whisper channel Task 250 just built. **Closed as reject-with-evidence, not deferred** (a task nobody will schedule is better killed explicitly — D-248's kill-or-commit spirit). Decision trail preserved: the borrow was RIGHT for their corpus (dated prose bullets, few cross-refs); ours inverts the economics. **Named re-open condition:** references captured in structured frontmatter (`refs:`) at WRITE time — a schema question, not a scanner question, and the note records that finding for whoever next reaches for a scanner.

## 2026-08-02 — D-415 · DECISION — One retrying `npm ci` across all 9 CI sites (the user approved the workflow change)

Five CI runs went red on registry/CDN blips (ENETUNREACH/ETIMEDOUT) in the week of 2026-07-29 with nothing wrong in the tree; because the standing rule is diagnose-from-logs-before-rerun, each cost a log read to confirm it was just the registry. A composite action (`.github/actions/npm-ci-retry`, 3 attempts, 10/20s backoff) now wraps every `npm ci` — 9 sites across 6 workflows — making the transient class self-healing and turning a run that fails all three attempts back into a real signal. **This is Task 250's noise threshold applied to our own CI**, which is why it earns a decision entry rather than a silent chore commit. `install-matrix.yml` held the only hand-rolled retry (npm's macOS Exit-handler bug) — converged into the shared action so there is one place to tune; the D-155 cache-restore-after-`npm ci` ordering is untouched (the retry wraps the install step only). Per the standing rule that workflow files are the user's call, this was proposed with its cost/benefit and built only on their explicit approval.

## 2026-08-02 — D-414 · DECISION — Task 255 grilled and ratified: the viewer's 8-point design (ephemeral read-only localhost server, API-first, five views)

**The second v0.6.4 grill ran** (5 questions + the user's own addition), alongside a fresh code-level prior-art read per the unconditional rule ([viewer survey](../research/2026-08-02-memory-viewer-prior-art.md) — claude-mem re-read at 89k★, its June-note snapshot stale; PAI/Pulse disambiguated from a name-collision project; hermes at 224k★ confirmed to have NO memory viewer, the differentiation evidence). **Ratified (canonical: design §24):** ephemeral loopback-only server (the datasette lifecycle — the field's resident daemons accrue a documented complaint class); JSON+HTML same routes, the server doubling as the kit's local read API — the user's own reasoning: "we can actually use it later for more things"; read-only STRUCTURALLY with copy-able `cmk forget/trust` commands as the answer to the field's #1 delete-from-viewer demand; five wave-1 views — the **decisions journal tab is the user's addition** (the evolution view `--scope decisions` can't give); all three tiers badged; manual refresh + freshness labels with **SSE filed as Task 259** (the user's requirement: a deferral becomes a TASK, not a note) carrying its named trigger; zero new deps (stdlib http + one HTML + vanilla JS — proven sufficient by the precedent's own post-bundle runtime); deferred stated: timeline, conflict-queue UI, stats. Build sub-tasks 255.1–255.5 filed with estimates.

## 2026-08-02 — D-413 · DECISION + NOTE — Task 250 SHIPPED (PR #334): the health whisper is live; the review round's three Blockings; two machine crashes cost only re-runs

**Shipped through the full two-pass split.** Implementer: 15 commits + 7 self-review findings fixed. Independent review: 3 Blocking + 2 Important + 3 Minor, all reproduced on real bins, all fixed — **B1** `index-drift` never cleared via its own prescribed fix (`reindex.mjs` now appends the `ok`; live-verify check pins run-the-fix-watch-it-clear), **B2** 16 KB tail eviction made sparse classes unreachable under real traffic (transition logging at high-cadence sites + 64 KB tail + the cadence-composition test), **B3** the skill's `Bash(cmk repair *)` grant exceeded design §23.5's claimed boundary — fixed under the arbitration's own escape clause by ungranting repair ENTIRELY: a `Bash()` prefix pattern cannot express `--index`-only without admitting `--index --hooks`, and a grant with a hole is worse than no grant. The directed question VINDICATED the implementer's fail-only spawn logging (the child's success appends the clearing `ok` — reproduced). Follow-up carried with trigger: extend `validate-skill-sources` to police per-skill grants (next skill-surface task, D-248). **The crash note (environment, not process):** kernel-power crashes on 07-29 and 08-02 each killed a background agent mid-verification — the 07-29 one had been mis-read as a stream stall until the 08-02 event log showed both. ## 2026-07-28 — D-409 · DECISION + BUG — Task 253: all three obsidian-mind borrows built; the `compact` half of the injection split NARROWED on primary-source evidence; the live test caught two bugs the green suite could not

**The three riders shipped** (design §20.9 for a+b, §8.5 for c): **(a)** source-aware injection split, **(b)** the injection-size meter, **(c)** `tmpdir()` cwd on all four detached-spawn sites. Zero LLM, zero deps, fail-open throughout.

**(a) `source` VERIFIED — twice, and the borrow was NARROWED.** Primary source (code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, read 2026-07-27) documents SessionStart's `source` as `startup` | `resume` | `clear` | `compact` | **`fork`** — a 5th value the 2026-07-21 research note's four-value list predates. Live-captured from a real `claude -p` run on this machine through a real SessionStart hook: `{"session_id":…,"source":"startup"}` then, on `--resume` of the same id, `…"source":"resume"` — the discriminator is real, not doc-only. **The narrowing:** obsidian-mind pointers on BOTH `resume` and `compact`; the kit pointers on **`resume` only**. Anthropic's own docs describe compaction as summarizing older history to free space, so the pre-compaction snapshot survives as a SUMMARY, not verbatim text — a pointer at content that may have been summarized away could silently cost a session its memory, which is the one failure this kit exists to prevent. Compaction is precisely when re-anchoring earns its bytes. `fork` / unknown / absent / unparseable all fail open to the full snapshot. **SETTLED — RATIFIED by the lead 2026-07-29 at review.** This is a deliberate deviation from the filed task text ("on resume/compact"); it is decided, not pending. Do not re-open without new evidence (per the decision-log discipline) — the evidence that would reopen it is proof that a pre-compaction injection survives compaction verbatim.

**The static/volatile split** (conservative, per the task's own "when in doubt, keep injecting"): REPLACED = the tier blocks + the state-label instruction that only describes labels inside them. KEPT = the authoritative-memory preamble (the D-40 under-fire class), the existence advertisement (§20.8 — worth MORE when the bulk is absent, not less), and the volatile action lines (temporal mention, commit proposal — a resumed session has not seen the git dirtiness that accrued since it started).

**(b) placement resolves the self-reference question by removing it.** The meter rides `systemMessage` (the D-130 user-display channel), never `additionalContext`: zero model tokens, the snapshot stays byte-identical (prefix cache + every byte-stability contract untouched), and it measures a string it is not part of, so the count is exact rather than "pre-meter". Door 5: `bytes` + `mode` land on the `recall.log` inject entry, so the token saving is measurable from the log instead of asserted in prose.

**BUG (found by the live test, not the suite) — the same mistake twice, in both directions.** The first cut derived "what the model can see" from the EMITTED text. In pointer mode that is wrong by construction: the model's memory is in its replayed context, not in this emission. Two live symptoms on the real bin, both invisible to 84 green unit tests: (1) the status line read **"memory is empty — capture starts this session"** on a resumed session holding a loaded fact — false, and alarming precisely to the user the D-130 status line exists to reassure; (2) the recall log wrote **`ids: []`**, telling the Task-190/192 learn-loop that a resumed session recalled nothing and under-attributing every downstream signal in it. Fixed by keeping `fullSnapshot` alongside the emission: the fact count and the attribution ids read the FULL text, only the meter reads what was sent. Both now regression-pinned. **Why the suite could not see it:** every unit fixture asserted on the snapshot string, and the two surfaces that disagree with it (a user-facing line and a diagnostic log) were only checked in full mode — the live-test rule's exact thesis, one more time.

**(c) the sweep is complete and caller-mapped both ways:** `capture-turn`→auto-extract, `inject-context`→compress-lazy (**both** branches, including the `shell:true` corrupt-install fallback), and `cmk-precompact` (npm **and** plugin twins)→precompact-worker. No child reads its cwd — each takes the root from `argv` and/or `CMK_PROJECT_DIR`, and every argv path is absolute; live-verified by driving the real Stop-hook bin and having the detached child report `process.cwd()` = tmpdir with `CMK_PROJECT_DIR` intact. Each shape now lives in ONE exported descriptor so it is Door-3 assertable without a real spawn — and the precompact twins stop carrying two copies of the same options object. **The guard that keeps it safe:** tmpdir is pinned only when a non-empty `projectRoot` is present, because each child's last-resort fallback is `process.cwd()` and re-pointing that at tmpdir with no root to hand over would scaffold a memory tree in the temp dir.

## 2026-07-28 — D-408 · DECISION + FIX — Task 252 shipped (PR #330): the premise falsified, six real PII bugs fixed instead — and the stress-flake class ROOT-CAUSED (D-406's trigger fired and paid)

**The falsification (supersedes D-395's by-product claim).** Task 252 was filed on D-395's note that dotted email local-parts slip `maskPii`. FALSE — dotted parts have masked since the pattern's first commit; the claim reasoned from "a test passed for the wrong reason" to a conclusion about the regex without ever running the regex (the falsifying check was one `node -e`; three independent proofs recorded in the task entry). D-395's text stays frozen; this entry is the correction its CHANGELOG citation now points at.

**What the audit actually found (all fixed, two by the reviewer):** (1) THE live leak — non-ASCII emails half-masked (`añ«EMAIL»`) by ASCII classes + ASCII `\b`; fixed with `\p{L}/\p{N}/\p{M}` + explicit lookarounds covering NFC AND NFD (decomposed macOS `ls`/`git` output is a real transcript source — the reviewer's Blocking catch); (2) the extension-exemption regression the first fix introduced (`john.doe@corp.com.csv` leaking — strip-and-retest-stem closed it); (3–4) dotted/international phone shapes + case-sensitive username masking; (5) the `mightContainPii` pre-filter acting as a silent GATE disabling patterns whose trigger chars it lacked — now self-detecting via the `PII_PATTERNS` registry whose bare samples are loop-tested; (6) a self-caught quadratic domain regex on the hook path (2508ms→1ms at cap, cost-test-pinned). Rulings: `git@`/`hg@` any-host allowlisted; real-TLD extensions never exempt; quoted-local/bare-E.164/3-3-4-number residuals named, tested, and traded in the masking direction where no discriminating signal exists. Sonar round: every refactored regex proven `.source`-byte-identical (9 entries + the per-call pattern).

**D-406's named trigger FIRED and was honored — the months-long stress mystery solved.** The gate bound (pii-patterns is hook surface), the first run went 4/5 on the recorded class, and the mandated detour found the root cause: **`hookTimeout` left at vitest's 10s default beside the raised 30s `testTimeout`** — subprocess-heavy `beforeEach` hooks legitimately cross 10s under full-suite load (11.3s measured; the identical failure reproduced at `--hookTimeout=1`; a 320×8 raw-syscall probe ruled out fs/git flakiness — which is why the class hit a DIFFERENT file each occurrence). One config line, evidence in-comment; stress 5/5 clean immediately after. The "known Windows cold-start flake" framing that survived since PR #22 dies here — it was a config gap all along. 3665 green.

**The ship (four rounds, one PR).** `stripBom` in front of `frontmatter.parse` made BOM'd facts visible kit-wide — and the caller-map found the real stakes: `writeFact`'s collision guard read a BOM'd fact as id-less and the next same-slug capture SILENTLY DESTROYED it (reproduced on the real bin; falsified per D-366 before being called data loss). The Sonar gate closed with condition-coverage tests that pin real contracts (the one uncovered branch is the repair guard's unreachable-by-construction arm — documented, not fabricated, per D-388). 3594 green · pytest 161 · parity OK.

The D-401 explicit-paths rule had overcorrected into cherry-picking. **The synthesis, now binding: `context/` is ONE CONSISTENCY UNIT — memory flushes stage the whole tier or none of it; explicit paths govern everything OUTSIDE the tier.** Healed by a complete flush; both heads green.

## 2026-07-27 — D-404 · FIX — Task 257: `frontmatter.parse` is BOM-tolerant kit-wide; the caller map found a silent CLOBBER the invisibility was hiding

_(Branch `task-257-bom-blindness`, awaiting the lead's review + merge. D-number assumed next-free — renumber if the lead has allocated 404 elsewhere.)_

**The one line, made total.** `parse` now strips a leading UTF-8 BOM via the canonical `read-json.mjs` `stripBom` and returns the stripped text on **every** branch (frontmatter, no-frontmatter, both parseError branches), so no caller can receive a BOM back and re-persist it; `format` never emits one, including a BOM a caller put in the body itself. Together those two invariants mean a BOM'd file **heals on its next legitimate rewrite** (trust / redact / merge / supersede) rather than needing a repair pass — the design-§4 "input boundary" contract, alongside the D-126 CRLF tolerance the same regex already carried. The scratchpad walk does not go through `parse` (bullets carry HTML-comment provenance), so `parseObservationsFromScratchpad` strips independently — the first line's `##` heading and bullet regex were blind, degrading `heading_path` to the bare filename. Content sha1s deliberately stay over the RAW bytes: adding or removing a BOM IS a file change and must invalidate a change-detection key.

**What the caller map was FOR — a data-loss bug, not just invisibility.** The 20-importer sweep is what turned this from a recall bug into a durability bug. `writeFact` refuses to overwrite a file already sitting at `<type>_<slug>.md` under a different id — but that guard is `readExistingFactId(path) !== null`, and `parse` returned `null` for a BOM'd file. Guard skipped; `findExistingFactById`'s dedup scan blind to the same file; `writeFileSync` **clobbered a real fact**. A hand-edit in a Windows editor was enough to make the next `cmk remember` destroy the fact it landed on. Falsified before being called data-loss, per D-366: reproduced **serially**, zero concurrency, with the mechanism traced through the code and both sides captured — pre-fix `action:'created'` (the file overwritten), post-fix `errorCategory:'collision'` with the original bytes intact. Pinned by test either way.

**Hash stability: Node PINNED (not changed), Python FIXED to match.** The ruling was "canonicalize must be BOM-insensitive" — Node already is, because JS `\s` matches U+FEFF (ECMA-262 folds `<ZWNBSP>` into WhiteSpace), so the collapse + trim stages absorb a leading BOM and a BOM'd fact derives the SAME id as its clean twin. Node was not touched; no existing fixture vector moved; the property is now a test instead of an accident.

**The twin diverged, and the lead ruled: fix it here.** Python's `re` `\s` does NOT match U+FEFF (category `Cf`, not whitespace), so `generate_id('P', BOM+text)` returned `P-95ZT9JAH` where Node returned `P-S79MJHFN` — a silent cross-implementation id fork that `canonicalize-parity` had never exercised, because **no vector carried a BOM**. The lead's call (2026-07-27): **Node is the reference implementation; its BOM-insensitivity is shipped behavior; a permanent id fork is exactly what the parity harness exists to prevent.** Python now folds U+FEFF as whitespace.

**The semantic choice — FOLD, not a leading strip.** Node does not special-case the *leading* position; it treats U+FEFF as whitespace in EVERY `\s`. A strip-the-first-BOM shim would have passed the leading-BOM case and still diverged on three real shapes: `"foo<U+FEFF>bar"` (Node `"foo bar"`), `"hello<U+FEFF>"` (Node `"hello"`), and `"-<U+FEFF>hello"` (Node strips the bullet marker → `"hello"`). Only folding keeps ALL other behavior identical, which is the actual contract. Implemented as one shared class, `_WS = r"[\s\ufeff]"`, applied to exactly the two stages a BOM can still reach — the bullet marker (runs BEFORE the collapse) and the collapse itself. `str.strip()` and `_RE_TRAILING_PUNCT_WS` are deliberately LEFT ALONE: by the time they run the collapse has already turned every U+FEFF into a plain space, so widening them would be a provable no-op, and a no-op edit to a byte-exact cross-implementation contract is noise.

**Pinned by seven vectors (38 → 45)**, two of which exist specifically to distinguish fold-from-strip (`bom-mid-string-folds-as-whitespace`, `bom-between-bullet-marker-and-text`). Written as `\uFEFF` JSON escapes, never literal bytes — an invisible character in a fixture is one an editor can silently eat. Verified by the workflow's own three commands: `vitest tests/canonicalize.test.js` **109 passed** (unchanged — Node already handled every new vector), `pytest -q` **161 passed**, `scripts/parity-check.mjs` **OK — 45 vectors, byte-identical Node ≡ Python**. Mutation-confirmed: removing the fold turns **14 pytest cases red** and fails parity on canonical + all three tier ids.

**Residual divergence, measured and NOT changed.** The two `\s` classes still disagree in the OTHER direction: Python's `\s` matches **U+001C–U+001F and U+0085 (NEL)**; JavaScript's does not. No vector covers those, so the contract is untested there. Named rather than silently widened — same reasoning that sent the BOM question to the lead in the first place: that is a contract decision, not an implementation detail.

**Also in the change:** `memory-recovery.mjs` carried its own byte-identical copy of `stripBom` — the exact shared-module drift the rule exists to stop — now the canonical import. Its call sites are unchanged: the strip in front of `parse` is redundant-but-harmless, and the strip in front of the **textual** id splice is still load-bearing (that path never goes through `parse`).

**Review round — the lead chose to WIDEN, not soften.** The reviewer traced the blindness into a surface the first pass had classed as "read-only walk, pure gain": `inject-context`'s `readTierBlock` reads scratchpads with a bare `readFileSync`, so a hand-edited BOM travelled **verbatim into the injected session snapshot** — a raw U+FEFF in the model's context (reproduced at snapshot bytes 2064→2067). The real damage was one level down: `truncateTierToBudget` splits sections on `/^##\s/`, which a BOM'd heading LINE fails, so the section was **not a boundary** — it fell into the always-kept preamble and became **un-droppable**, INVERTING the §20.3 value-ordered eviction (a low-trust section surviving while a high-trust one is evicted to make room). Pre-existing, and now fixed: one `stripBom` at the read boundary, which repairs all three downstream readers (`collectBulletValues`, `hasRealContent`, the truncator) at once — the strip belongs at the read, not in three regexes.

**The one-strip-everywhere ruling.** `classifyFactId` was calling `parse(stripBom(text))` — a DOUBLE strip once `parse` gained its own. The asymmetry was the worst of both worlds: a 2-BOM file classified `valid`, so recovery certified as healthy a file the rest of the kit still could not read — invisible forever AND never flagged. The lead's ruling: **one strip everywhere.** Classification (and the universal repair guard) now use the same `parse` contract as every other reader, so a ≥2-BOM file is `not-derivable` → quarantined with its ORIGINAL bytes → named in the report. *Visible-and-flagged beats invisible-forever*, and the user keeps every byte on both paths.

**The third collision path, pinned.** Same id at a DIFFERENT slug rides `findExistingFactById` → `eachFactIn` → `parse`, never `readExistingFactId`. The reviewer verified it was already correct post-fix; it is now a contract test (`duplicate-elsewhere` + `recurrenceCount` bump + no second file) rather than an accident.

**And the hazard this entry itself was committing.** Ten prose examples across DECISION-LOG / design.md / tasks.md carried **literal invisible U+FEFF bytes** — the exact editor-can-eat failure D-404 warns about, in the write-up of D-404. Replaced with the visible `<U+FEFF>` notation (and the `_WS` references now quote the real source form, `r"[\s\ufeff]"`). Deliberately NOT touched: D-402's own entry and the older Cursor-hook entry (frozen records — history, not drift), and `tasks.md`'s `INVISIBLE_CHARS` catalog, where literal invisible characters ARE the content.

**Verification.** 46 tests in `tests/cli-frontmatter-bom.test.js` across four doors, including Door 3 (the REAL `cmk reindex --full` + `cmk search` bins as subprocesses) against a **committed real-payload fixture** — captured from actual Windows PowerShell 5.1 `Set-Content -Encoding utf8`, so it carries BOM **and** CRLF, pinned `-text` in `.gitattributes` because `*.md text eol=lf` would otherwise rewrite the CRLF half away; a test asserts the on-disk bytes so losing that rule fails the suite instead of quietly degrading the fixture. Three mutation confirmations: reverting the `parse` strip turns **16** tests red (incl. the clobber test and the real-bin test), reverting the `format` strip turns 1 red, reverting the scratchpad strip turns 2 red.

## 2026-07-25 — D-403 · DECISION — Task 248 shipped (PR #326): install recovers what the old bug stranded; the review round that caught corrupt-then-evict BEFORE it reached a user; Task 257 (BOM blindness) filed

**The ship.** `cmk install` now auto-recovers pre-246 orphaned tiers (bounded pruned scan, live-state discriminator, NEIGHBOR-PROJECT guard — a candidate whose parent has its own `.git`/managed block is never touched or hinted at), relocates facts byte-identically (SHA-verified; ids+dates preserved; collision-safe against live+tombstoned+superseded+bullet-tombstoned ids), repairs malformed/id-less facts via content-derived ids with quoted `legacy_id`, quarantines the unrepairable with ORIGINAL bytes preserved, and stays fail-open end to end — recovery can never break an install. HC-13 is the advisory backstop (12→13 rippled; historical cut-gate banners RESTORED to their true numbers under dated markers). 3528 tests; stress 5/5 first invocation. **Proof on the real repo the same hour:** the shipped installer repaired `project_resume-v031-cutgate-near-complete.md` (invalid `P-RES031CG` → derived `P-QT3B3ZB7`, `legacy_id` kept) — the file recall has been blind to since Task 232 flagged it is finally indexed, and the per-boot rescan cost is gone.

**The review round (the split's run #5, its most important):** the reviewer REPRODUCED two Blocking install-path bugs — (B1) an empty `id:` value took the insert path → duplicate YAML key → corrupt file → next run evicted the mangled bytes (corrupt-then-evict on the likeliest wild shape); (B2) a nested legitimately-installed project read as a stray, its facts copied out, and install PRINTED A DELETE HINT for a live memory tier — the exact outcome the guardrail lineage exists to prevent. Fixes: the universal `repairFactText` guard (re-parse + id match + body-untouched before ANY write; failure quarantines the original) and the neighbor guard (lead's scope ruling: parent project markers = neighbor, fails safe). Plus: `$&` replacement-string mangling, the depth off-by-one that the budget validator had passed VACUOUSLY (now registered; gate-bite verified by mutation), and the record-vs-drift restoration of historical banners. Twelve findings, twelve fixed, one round.

**Filed: Task 257** — `frontmatter.parse` is BOM-blind kit-wide (a BOM'd fact file silently ceases to exist; 248's live test found it; deliberately NOT fixed mid-task on the install path — the parser is the most-shared module in the tree and deserves its own caller-mapped pass). v0.6.3 rider per the anti-stalling default.

## 2026-07-25 — D-402 · DECISION + FIX — Task 248 implemented: install auto-recovers orphaned memory tiers AND repairs id-less fact files; doctor gets HC-13 as the backstop it was reframed into

**The ship** (branch `task-248-install-auto-recover`, awaiting the lead's review + merge). One new module — [`packages/cli/src/memory-recovery.mjs`](../../packages/cli/src/memory-recovery.mjs) — with three public boundaries (`scanStrayTiers` for the detection HC-13 shares, `recoverMemory` for the pass, `formatRecoveryReport` for the rendering), wired into `install()` so every install/upgrade run carries it with **no command for the user** (the D-169 automatic-path criterion). Both classes of pre-existing damage are repaired in one pass: **orphaned `context/` tiers** left by the pre-246 cwd bug (D-389), and **fact files with a missing or non-`ID_PATTERN` id** that `index-rebuild` skips *without checkpointing*, so they are re-scanned every boot and invisible to recall (D-394).

**Design decisions worth ratifying.**

1. **Depth bound = 4.** A stray is created at the AGENT'S CWD, and the realistic deep cwd is a monorepo package or one level inside it (`packages/cli/src` = 3). The pruned walk (`node_modules` / every dot-directory / build-vendor-template trees) is what makes it cheap enough to run on every install AND every doctor.
2. **Recovery COPIES, never moves.** That is what makes the pass idempotent by construction — a second run finds everything already at root and collision-skips it — and it means the husk is never damaged. The user deletes it (ADR-0018 / D-193), with a `platform-commands` delete hint printed for their shell.
3. **The collision id-set includes `archive/tombstones/` + `archive/superseded/`.** The sharpest edge in the feature: copying a stray twin of a tombstone back into `memory/` would **silently resurrect a `forget`** — a worse outcome than the stranding this fixes.
4. **The install report prints ONLY what the run actioned**, so a re-install over an already-recovered husk is completely silent; the standing "this folder can go" reminder is HC-13's job. That is the reframe (P-3PWCGWZH — *"doctor should only be part of the fix, not the actual fix"*) made concrete: **install fixes, doctor reminds.**
5. **Quarantine lives at `<factDir>/archive/quarantine/`**, not a new top-level `memory/quarantine/`: `archive/` is already the tier's "not a live fact, still kept" area (tombstones + superseded), `template/project/memory/archive/` already scaffolds the parent, every fact walk skips subdirectories, and `redact.mjs` already walks the fact dir recursively — so a quarantined file stays inside a leak scrub's reach. A new top-level dir would have had to earn all four.
6. **Id repair is a TEXTUAL edit, never a YAML round-trip.** `format(parse(text))` would re-quote and re-order unrelated frontmatter, which is precisely what faithful relocation (P-9W7XDMCA) forbids. A missing id is inserted after the opening `---`; an invalid id is swapped with the old value kept as `legacy_id`, so a citation of the old id still greps to the file. BOM and EOL style preserved as found.

**FAIL-OPEN is structural, not prose.** `recoverMemory` returns an error result rather than throwing, and its outcome **never joins `install()`'s `errors` array** — so a recovery failure cannot set the install's exit code. Proven with a real-bin test (a stray whose `memory` is a FILE, so `readdir` throws `ENOTDIR`): install still exits 0.

**Verification.** 46 tests in `tests/cli-memory-recovery.test.js` across four doors, including a Door-3 run of the REAL `cmk.mjs install` bin as a subprocess. The two highest-stakes assertions were **mutation-confirmed** — disabling the id-collision check and appending one byte on write each turned the suite red on exactly the right tests. Live-verified sandboxed on Windows against the real bin with a planted stray + a malformed fact + a template-shaped decoy: SHA-256-identical recovery, husk intact, decoy untouched, `INDEX.md` rebuilt with both facts, quarantine populated, second run silent with no duplicate, `cmk doctor` = 13 checks with HC-13 WARN naming the husk.

**One finding routed, not buried: a UTF-8 BOM makes a fact file invisible to the whole kit.** The first live-test pass wrote its fixtures with PowerShell `Set-Content -Encoding utf8` (which emits a BOM on 5.1), and `INDEX.md` came back EMPTY — `frontmatter.parse`'s `^---` anchor does not tolerate a leading `﻿`, so a BOM'd fact file parses as "no frontmatter" and is dropped by reindex, recall, and every fact walk. PRE-EXISTING and kit-wide (the D-306 BOM class, one level in), not caused by Task 248: the kit's own writers never emit a BOM, so producing one takes a hand-edit in a Windows editor. Deliberately NOT fixed here — `parse()` is the most-shared module in the tree and widening it mid-task on the install path is exactly the scope creep this task cannot afford. `memory-recovery` strips the BOM for CLASSIFICATION only and preserves it on disk. **Filed for the lead to lane:** the fix is one line (`stripBom`, already exported by `read-json.mjs`, in front of `parse`'s regex) plus a caller-map over every fact/scratchpad reader.

**The review round — 2 Blocking + 4 Important + 6 Minor, every one reproduced, every one fixed.** The two Blocking findings are the ones worth recording, because they are the same shape twice: **a discriminator that answered ONE question when the situation had two.**

**B1 — corrupt-then-evict.** `applyIdRepair` chose INSERT-vs-SWAP from the PARSED id value, but `id:` with an empty value and `id: null` both parse to `null` exactly like a MISSING id. So the most likely wild shape got a SECOND `id:` line appended — a duplicate YAML key, unparseable, WRITTEN ANYWAY — and the next install then quarantined the mangled bytes. The user would have lost the original to a pass whose entire purpose is not losing things. Two-part fix: the branch now keys off the `id:` LINE, and — the half that matters more — **a UNIVERSAL GUARD re-parses the repaired text and requires it to carry the derived id with the body intact BEFORE any write**, demoting a failure to the ordinary not-derivable → quarantine path so the ORIGINAL bytes are what survives. Deliberately a guard rather than a third special case: the empty-`id:` bug was ONE unhandled shape, and the next one would have shipped the identical outcome — it already exists (a block-scalar `id: |` whose continuation lines cannot be spliced), and the guard now catches it.

**B2 — recovery could eat a NEIGHBOUR's memory.** The scan asked only "does this tier hold live state?" — but a nested REAL project (a second repo checked out inside the first; or the fat-finger case of running `cmk install` from a folder that holds several installed repos) presents a tier full of live state that is **indistinguishable from a stray when viewed from inside the tier**. It copied that project's facts into the wrong project's memory and printed a delete hint for a LIVE tier. The evidence has to come from the PARENT: an own `.git` entry (dir or file, so worktrees count), or an agent instruction file carrying the `core-memory-kit:start` managed block. Skipped inside `scanStrayTiers` so recovery AND HC-13 both go silent — a neighbour is not "a stray we decline to fix", it is not a stray. This check **fails SAFE, not fail-open** (cannot tell → assume neighbour): a false neighbour costs one un-recovered stray the user can still move by hand; a false stray costs another project its memory.

**The Importants, briefly.** A `**Doc ripple (the 12→13 counts walk).**` inside an old id was a `String.replace` substitution pattern and mangled the output — now a replacer FUNCTION with a single-line-coerced, quoted value. A repaired BOM'd file still could not be seen by reindex while the report claimed "now indexed and recallable", so the pass now **strips the BOM on a file it is already rewriting**, making the claim true instead of softening the wording. `MAX_SCAN_DEPTH` was off by one — the documented 4 found only 3 — and it was invisible because **only the over-cap side had a test**; both sides are now pinned and the budget is registered in `validate-budget-pairs`, which had been passing vacuously over it. And the first round's count normalization had overwritten HISTORICAL cut-gate banners with numbers those cuts never had: restored to 11 with dated ignore markers, the same RELEASE-PLAN precedent from the first round now applied to the lines that first round got wrong.

**The Minors.** The SECOND tombstone directory (`<tierRoot>/archive/tombstones/`, where `forget` retires a scratchpad BULLET — a different path from the fact archive, one level up) was missing from the collision census, so a forgotten bullet could return as a fact file. The renderer could throw after a successful install (now total — an absurd way to lose fail-open). The liveness probe read whole files to learn "non-empty" and paid it on every `cmk doctor`, where a `transcripts/` dir can be tens of megabytes (now a stat). `design.md` carried its own pre-existing HC-count drift (`HC-1..HC-8`, "Eight yes/no checks"). The test count was stale in two places.

**The meta-lesson worth keeping: both Blocking bugs were shapes my own green suite could not see, because my fixtures were the shapes I had already thought of.** The reviewer's contribution was not spotting bad code — it was supplying INPUTS I had not imagined (an empty `id:`, a nested repo). The structural answer to that is not more fixtures; it is the universal guard, which makes the unimagined shape safe without having to enumerate it first.

**Doc ripple (the 12→13 counts walk).** HEALTH-CHECKS.md (row + self-repair section) · design §13.2 (new — the mechanism) · docs/CLI.md (install behavior) · memory-lifecycle-map (a repair path, not a new stage) · glossary (**Stray tier** / **Faithful relocation** / **Quarantine**) · README + packages/cli/README (one capability line each — an upgrade that rescues lost memory IS user-visible) · CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` · the HC-count ripple across QUICKSTART / both READMEs / glossary / CLI.md / four cut-gate guides. **Byproduct, stated rather than silently fixed:** the cut-gate guides carried PRE-EXISTING check-count drift the counts validator structurally cannot see (a bare "11 checks" sitting next to a range it CAN see — the exact ECC "checked 40 places, the drift landed in the 41st" shape, one notation-level in). Every `cmk doctor` count in those live gate docs is now 13, because a gate whose PASS criterion reads "11 checks" fails the next cut for the wrong reason. `docs/RELEASE-PLAN.md`'s Task-120 line went the OTHER way: its range had been drifted FORWARD by an earlier sweep, so it was restored to the historical `HC-1..HC-7` with an ignore marker — a shipped-record line is history, not drift.

## 2026-07-23 — D-400 · DECISION — Task 256 filed + laned v0.6.3: anchor co-citation edges ("graphify for memories") — the sparse-graph diagnosis, and why 232's skip doesn't bind

## 2026-07-23 — D-399 · FIX — `.obsidian/` gitignored at any depth: Task 254's first REAL use (the user, minutes after ship) found the gap the whole two-pass cycle missed

**The find:** the user opened the repo as an Obsidian vault to see the new MAP.md graph — and Obsidian wrote an untracked `.obsidian/` workspace-config folder that NOTHING excluded. Worse than the root case: opening the RECOMMENDED `context/memory/` vault would put `.obsidian/` (with its constantly-churning `workspace.json`) inside the committed memory tier, where every `git add context/` memory flush would stage per-user editor state into the public repo. `docs/OBSIDIAN.md` even said "the kit ships no `.obsidian/` folder" — true, but it guarded the wrong direction: the USER's Obsidian ships one into the vault it opens.

**The fix:** `.obsidian/` in `.gitignore`, unanchored (matches any depth — verified against both the root and `context/memory/` paths).

**The lesson (the live-test rule's purest instance yet):** implementer + reviewer + Sonar + 3436 tests all passed over this, because no automated pass OPENS the app — the gap was structurally invisible to every gate and took the first human minutes to hit. Same class as D-303/D-306: the input boundary only the real world exercises. The user's screenshot doubled as the first real-world verification that the vault view works (the memory ball rendered, MAP.md as hub).

## 2026-07-22 — D-395 · DECISION + NOTE — Task 233 shipped (PR #322): recall gets its evidence-bearing hint AND its instrument; the review round catches a Blocking privacy leak; the D-390 trial verdict firms up at n=2

**The ship.** ADR-0024's three composed mitigations are live: `buildMemoryHint` static→FTS5 (≥20-char gate, bm25 floor −0.5 named+tunable per the Octopoda calibration lesson, ≤3 index lines never bodies, fail-open, **8.4–9.8 ms measured on the real 2007-fact corpus with screening included**); the SessionStart existence advertisement (markdown+logs only — the §20.3 pin held); the Memora ladder refinements in both SKILL.md copies. The ADR's success criterion is now measurable; the before/after comparison starts accruing from this ship.

**The review round's Blocking catch — the class that matters most for this product:** the first implementation logged the RAW prompt as the hint's `query`, and the reviewer REPRODUCED `<private>` content landing verbatim in recall.log (FR-15/§6.6 violation — the bin sanitizes a downstream copy, not the payload the hint saw). Fixed at the root: the prompt is screened (`sanitizePrivacyTags` + `maskPii`) BEFORE any use, so private terms never reach even the FTS query; the log stores capped screened terms only; the repro is a permanent regression test. **Two by-products, both kept honest:** (1) a PRE-EXISTING kit-wide `maskPii` gap (dotted email local-parts) — filed as **Task 252** with a trigger, not buried; the implementer also strengthened its own weak test that had passed for the wrong reason. **The D-390 trial at n=2 (adoption evidence):** two full lead→implementer→reviewer cycles; the reviewer caught an Important runtime-cost bug (232's cold-edge non-termination) and a Blocking privacy leak (233), BOTH by reproduction, both invisible to the implementer's green suites; all fix rounds resolved by resuming the same implementer with context intact. Cost: ~2.3M subagent tokens across both tasks against a lead context that ran the whole lane without compaction. Standing verdict: **helpful — the split works, with the reviewer leg on the strong tier being the load-bearing choice.** Formal CLAUDE.md graduation can wait for the user's read, but the evidence bar ("same quality at lower lead-token cost, measured") is met.

## 2026-07-22 — D-393 · NOTE — Octopoda-OS code-dived (the user's "why not research now?"): real loop-detection prior art for Tasks 250/212; the rare inverse of README-inflation

**Provenance + method:** the user flagged the repo, then pushed "why not do the research now?" — correct, because Task 250's grill REQUIRES outward research (D-375) and this feeds it directly. Run as the model-split's first research delegation (opus agent, shallow clone of v3.3.5, D-364 discipline: every claim tagged CODE-VERIFIED/README-ONLY/PARTIAL with file paths). Note: `docs/research/2026-07-22-octopoda-os-code-dive.md`.

**Headline:** the loop-detection engine is REAL and ships MORE than claimed — 10 deterministic stdlib-only classifiers behind a "five-signal" README (the inverse of the mem0/MemOS pattern). Honest caveats: zero unit tests on the v2 classifiers, every precision/recall figure unmeasured (`None`), two conflated engines (the inline v1 is what actually auto-pauses), and a server-shaped runtime (Postgres+pgvector+RLS) the kit ignores wholesale.

**What the kit takes (recorded on the task entries):** (1) reflection.py's pattern-aware FALSE-POSITIVE SUPPRESSOR (known-benign key shapes — rolling summaries, heartbeats, recovery markers — suppress the alert) = shipped precedent for Task 250's noise-threshold amendment; (2) pure-classifiers-over-an-event-log = the buildable shape for Task 212, with the honest gap that retry/nondeterminism detection needs a tool-call log the kit doesn't write yet; (3) their SDK-side auto-injection (no tool-nudging at all) independently validates the kit's SessionStart-injection approach, and their relevance-floor calibration lesson (0.80 filtered 5/7 relevant facts → lowered to 0.45) is a warning for our snapshot/hint thresholds (Task 233); (4) the self-anchored hash-chain audit is a modest, honest model if Task 127's team tier ever wants tamper-evidence. Task 250's "Prior art: NONE" annotated: detection prior art now EXISTS; the self-heal-first repair-UX half of the corpus gap remains open for the grill.
